## Completion Requirements

This scaffold is not a completed review. Artifact review defaults to parallel subagents for the eight required lenses. The artifact-review workflow is explicit authorization to delegate those lenses. Only use `in-session-emulated` when subagents are unavailable or the human explicitly requested no delegation; if used, state that the review is not independently adversarial and treat it as weaker evidence. Completion requires every required reviewer row to be populated, each reviewer to have a verdict, blocking findings to be fixed and re-reviewed or explicitly human-accepted, and the aggregate verdict to be the actual artifact-review verdict returned by the reviewer set rather than a fixed example result.

## Findings

### Feasibility

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 85] [blocking] The `forked` row (§2 step 9 / §6) plus "idempotent by id — a row whose `id` already exists is not written again" means a parent that is still running at fork time never gets its real terminal row: when P later reaches `fixed`/`overflow`/`failed`, `RecordTerminal` sees the existing `forked` row (verdict NEEDS_REVISION, outcome "") and skips. P's verdict map is therefore wrong for every non-terminal fork, and `runchain.Resolve`'s escalation rule (`escalatedForTarget` is first-match over all rows, `runchain.go:155-165`) never sees P's `overflow`. Either the recorder must upgrade `forked → complete` (append a second row; `ChainTo` is last-wins via `byID`, `runchain.go:126-131`, so readers tolerate it) or the forked row must be omitted and `attemptNumber` derived another way — evidence: spec §2 step 9, §6 lines 107-116; `machine.go` `terminal()` is invoked on every terminal advance (`fail`, `finish`, terminal-branch of `advance`).
- [P1] [confidence 90] [blocking] `Export` redaction is defeated by its own `snapshot.json`: §5 says the exported snapshot is the **pre-redaction fold**, but `run.Snapshot` carries `Vars` (values), `NodeOutputs` (full `rejected[].desc`, full review-lenses findings), `TreeStatus`, `LastError.Detail`, `AllowedCmds[].Argv`, absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir` (`run/types.go:190-233`) — every field §5 redacts in `audit.jsonl`. Additionally the r2 preamble ("keeps every `node_output` (the snapshot must re-fold; `output_hash` is checked)") contradicts §5: after redacting `node_output.output`, `Apply` rejects the next `delta_applied` (`fold.go:120-121` `output_hash`), `gate.error.detail_summary` is an unknown field under `DisallowUnknownFields` (`fold.go:466`, `GateError` has no such field `types.go:138-143`), and `prev` chain breaks — the export cannot re-fold at all. Spec must pick one contract: redact the snapshot too (and state that the export is evidence-only, never re-foldable), or keep outputs verbatim.
- [P2] [confidence 85] [advisory] `Export` cannot copy `audit.torn-*.bin` through the declared seams: `Sidecar.List/Read` refuse any name starting with `audit.` (`sidecar.go:22-23`, `checkSidecarArgs` → `ERR_SIDECAR{name}`), and `RunStore` exposes no torn-file reader. The only route is direct FS under `Store.Root()`, which `MemSidecar`/`memStore` cannot exercise — F8 "torn files copied" is not reachable at 100% with the harness fakes without a new seam (e.g. `Sidecar.ReadRaw` or a `Store.TornFiles(runID)`).
- [P2] [confidence 80] [advisory] Step 7 only re-validates `node_output` payloads, but with `AcceptWorkflowChange` other copied events fail inside `Append`: a copied `cmd_call`/`overflow_handler` whose command was removed/renamed hits `unsanctioned_cmd` (`fold.go:164-171`, child `AllowedCmds` are the *new* list) and surfaces as `ERR_APPEND_REJECTED`, not `ERR_COPY_INVALID`; a copied `node_output` whose node name no longer exists has no kind to `Decode` with. Define `ERR_COPY_INVALID{seq, reason}` for these (and note the child dir is left behind in both cases).
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] Step 6's ancestor rule for `agent-edit` is hollow: `fix_baseline.Head` must equal `Snapshot.Head`, which the immediately preceding `tree{Head: HEAD}` sets to HEAD (`fold.go:212-216`, `:98-100`), and copied transitions with `Origin` never set `FixEntryHead` (`fold.go:202`). So `FixEntryHead == HEAD` always, and `commit_exists` counts `FixEntryHead..HEAD` (`gates.go:97-108`) — any commit made *before* the fork is invisible; "commit, then fork --from fix" yields `ERR_NO_COMMIT` again. F2 only tests commit-after-fork. Either document this ordering rule in spec 5's recovery recipe or drop the ancestor allowance (exact-head everywhere).
- [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] Checkpoint rule is ambiguous for looping workflows: `sdlc-loop` loops back to its initial state `discover` (`workflows/sdlc-loop.yaml:16`), so `--from discover` with `AtIter` nil is both "latest transition with To == discover" and "From == Initial → seq = 1". Likewise `ERR_VAR_FROZEN{name, state}` must define which state is reported when several ran and reference the var (`adjudicate` and `verify` both reference `$JUDGE`; F3 asserts `adjudicate`) — say "first in `w.States` order".
- [P3] [confidence 80] [advisory] Torn parent: `load` only sets `m.torn` (`machine.go` load), so Fork would create the whole child (Create, sidecar, copies, tree) and fail only at step 9 with `ERR_AUDIT_TORN` from `prepareAppend` (`store.go:119-121`), orphaning a child with no `fork` link. Refuse up front like `Advance`/`Record` do. Also "version-selected file" in §3 has no store support (single `audit.jsonl`, `Log.Version` fixed at 1, `store.go:77`); `VerifyOrigin` should define a reason for a parent whose chain fails to parse (`ERR_AUDIT_CHAIN`) — currently only `parent_missing|hash_mismatch`.
- [P3] [confidence 70] [advisory] `runchain.Resolve` requires every ancestor to share scope **and** `sameTarget` (DeepEqual, `runchain.go:70-73`); the fsm target id is `<workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>`, so a fork accepted with a different workflow name (`AcceptWorkflowChange`) or a child whose `init` records a different `base_sha` cannot resolve `--previous-run`, and `attemptNumber = 1 + parent's` silently degrades to 1 when the parent row is absent (`ReadRuns` default, `runchain.go:115-117`). Specify the target for forks (root's target?) and whether a mismatch is an error.
- [P3] [confidence 65] [follow-up] `RecordTerminal(repoRoot)` has no clock: `createdAt` comes from `View.Snapshot.CreatedAt` but `updatedAt` needs "now", forcing `time.Now` inside `machine` (every other timestamp is `Deps.Clock`). Take a `Clock` (or use `deps.Clock` by making it a method on `Deps`) so F9 can assert literal rows.

NOTES: Everything else checks out against the implemented exports: copied events keep `At/State/Iter/Node/Mock` and fold cleanly because `checkProvenance` (`fold.go:413-426`) requires `Origin{RunID==ParentRunID, Seq==ev.Seq, Version≥1, Hash!=""}` exactly on `2..ForkedAtSeq` and forbids it elsewhere, `mock_stamp` is bypassed for `Origin` events (`fold.go:85`), and `Log.Version` is 1 from `parseLines`; the step-8 `tree`→`fix_baseline` ordering satisfies `fix_baseline_kind/head/order`; `fork` is post-terminal-allowed (`fold.go:333-338`); `Resolve` preserves `Refs`/`CmdRefs` on the resolved copy (`resolve.go:64` `c := *w`) so `VarsReferencedBy` on the resolved workflow works; `NodesRun` holds node names so the freeze check needs a state↔node map (trivial via `w.Nodes`); `LineHash`, `OutputHash`, `Key`, `CapDetail`, `consentList` (same package), `Git.CommonDir/IsAncestor`, `gate.TreeHash`, `ResolveCmds`, `ValidateRunID`, `run.RunID` all exist as named; `Diff` rows align on `LLMCallData.Index` + `Event.Node` (set by the audit closure, `machine.go` `audit`) and `LLMCallData.Verdict/Confidence`; `cmdCalls` ordinals are recounted from the log on `load`, so copied `cmd_call`s count. `machine` importing `runchain`/`state` introduces no cycle (`run` already imports `state`). `metareview status` (`cmd/metareview/main.go:87-95`) does not read `runs.jsonl` today, so the row shape only needs to satisfy `runchain.Record` (string `previousRunId` accepts both `null` and `""`).

### Completeness

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [85] [blocking] `Export` §5 contradicts itself on redaction: every redaction rule is stated per *event* (`init.vars` → `sha256:` unless `IncludeVars`, `gate.error.detail` emptied, `tree.status` reduced, `repo_root`/`work_dir` relativised, `allowed_cmds[].argv` var values hashed), yet the same section writes `snapshot.json` as "the **pre-redaction** fold". `run.Snapshot` carries `Vars` (verbatim values), `TreeStatus` (full porcelain), `LastError.Detail` (up to `MaxDetail` = 64 KB of uncommitted diff), absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, and `AllowedCmds` argv — so an `IncludeVars=false` bundle still ships every var value and the last gate diff. An implementer cannot satisfy both sentences; SEC-13/SEC-7 (owned by this spec) are not actually closed. Define snapshot.json as a redacted projection (same field rules) or drop it and let `fsm export` consumers re-fold `audit.jsonl` (the spec already says the snapshot "must re-fold") — evidence: spec §5 lines 95-104; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:117-233` (`Vars`, `TreeStatus`, `LastError`, `RepoRoot`, `WorkDir`, `AllowedCmds`); run spec §12 row 3 ("redact `Vars` … `tree.status`").
- [P1] [80] [blocking] The `ESCALATED` brake has no producer. §6 says "`runchain.Resolve` applies the same-target escalation rule to forks of an escalated run" and F9 tests "`--previous-run` resolution via `runchain.Resolve`", but no step of `Fork` (§2 steps 1-10) or `RecordTerminal` calls `Resolve`, §7 lists no error for the refusal (`Resolve` returns plain `fmt.Errorf`, not an `ERR_*`), spec 5 §2 has no `--previous-run` on any `fsm` subcommand, and nothing states which run id is passed as `PreviousRunID` (the parent) or what happens when `attemptNumber` would exceed `maxAttempts` (existing writers force `ESCALATED` via `verdictForCounts`; this spec's verdict map never does). Owned items "`fsmRunRecord` + verdict map + `ESCALATED`" (ledger row 3, SCP3-1, CMP3-10) therefore have a mapping but no enforcement contract — evidence: spec §6 lines 115-117, §8 F9, §7; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:64-91` (errors on `previous run … already escalated` / `same target already escalated`); `internal/taskdone/review.go:161,450`; spec 5 §2 lines 24-40.
- [P2] [75] [advisory] `RecordTerminal` idempotency freezes a forked parent's verdict: step 9 writes a `forked` row (`verdict: NEEDS_REVISION`, `outcome: ""`) for a non-terminal P; §6 then says "a row whose `id` already exists is not written again", so when P later reaches `overflow` its row is never updated and never becomes `ESCALATED` (and `updatedAt`/`outcome` stay stale). `runchain.ChainTo` builds `byID` last-wins, so appending a second row for the same id is a safe upsert — specify "idempotent per `(id, status)`: a terminal row supersedes a `forked` row" and add an F9 row for fork-then-terminate — evidence: spec §2 step 9, §6 lines 107-116; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:126-130`.
- [P2] [80] [advisory] Torn-file copy has no seam: §5 copies "`audit.torn-*.bin`", but `Sidecar.List`/`Read` reject any `audit.*` name (`ValidSidecarName`) and `run.RunStore` exposes no method to read them, so `Export(ctx, deps Deps, …)` must reach into the filesystem behind both interfaces — untestable against `MemSidecar`/`NewMemStore` as F8 ("torn files copied") requires. Declare the producer (e.g. `RunStore.TornFiles(runID)` or a `Sidecar` relaxation) — evidence: spec §5 line 96, §8 F8; `internal/fsm/machine/sidecar.go:22-40,106-124`; `internal/fsm/run/store.go:51-60`; `internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go:229`.

### Scope and alignment

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] §6 writes a `forked` runs.jsonl row at fork time, contradicting locked C27 / plan §1.9 ("appended once, at terminal, by `advance`"), and the deviation is not ledgered anywhere (this spec has no ledger; the r2 header only says "a `forked` row so children can chain"). Combined with §6's own rule "Idempotent: a row whose `id` already exists is not written again", the parent's real terminal outcome is then never recorded: step 1 allows forking any transition checkpoint (a non-terminal, in-flight P included — "A terminal P is allowed" is stated as the special case), step 9 writes P a `forked`/`NEEDS_REVISION` row, and when P later reaches `fixed`/`clean` the machine's terminal call (spec 2 r4: "`Deps.Terminal` is idempotent and runs on every terminal advance") is a no-op. `metareview status` and `runchain` see a permanent NEEDS_REVISION for a passing run. Either the forked row must be updatable (breaking "idempotent by id") or it must not exist (honouring C27) — the spec currently claims both. — evidence: target §2 step 1 (l.36-40), step 9 (l.65-66), §6 (l.107-117); plan §0 C27, §1.9 (l.321-326); spec 2 r4 header "Deps.Terminal is idempotent and runs on every terminal advance"; spec 2 §7 M9.
- [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] §6's verdict map sends `overflow` → **ESCALATED**, but plan §1.6 (the mapping C27/§1.9 binds to: `"verdict": <§1.6 mapping>`) locks `overflow` → NEEDS_REVISION, and the change is unledgered. It is not a cosmetic relabel: `target.id = <workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>` is inherited verbatim by every fork (step 7 copies `InitData` incl. `BaseSHA`), so an ESCALATED parent row makes `runchain.escalatedForTarget` match every fork and every re-`init` on the same base; under CLAUDE.md's completion rule ("ESCALATED stops same-target retries") the design's prescribed recovery from a safety stop (design §10.1 resume, §16 overflow handler = "fallback action") becomes a forbidden same-target retry. The accompanying sentence "`runchain.Resolve` applies the same-target escalation rule to forks of an escalated run" is not traceable to any step: nothing in §2 calls `Resolve`, spec 5's fork command has no `--previous-run`, and the only `Resolve` mention is the F9 test row — so the claimed enforcement does not exist while the ESCALATED label does. — evidence: target §6 (l.115-117), §8 F9; plan §1.6 table (l.275-286), §1.9 (l.323); `internal/runchain/runchain.go:49-92,155-165`; CLAUDE.md "Completion Rule"; spec 5 §2 fork command line.
- [P2] [confidence 50] [advisory] The var-freeze rule is narrower than plan §1.7.4 (FEA-N3/INT-10) and the dropped half is unrecorded. Plan: `K` is frozen "if any replayed `llm_call`/`cmd_call` used it **or any `cmd:` references it**". Target step 3 freezes only `K ∈ VarsReferencedBy(s)` for `s ∈ NodesRun`; `NodesRun` lists only nodes that produced `node_output` (run spec §4.2), and `VarsReferencedBy` covers only the node's own `cmd` (`workflow.go:744-750`). So a convergence `cmd` atom or `on_overflow` handler that ran in the copied prefix with `$K` in its argv does not freeze `K`; step 5's re-consent lets an operator change it, after which the copied `cmd_call`s carry the old argv yet still "count toward the child's ordinals" (l.62) against the new `AllowedCmds`. Run spec §11 ledgers the `vars_used`→`NodesRun` swap but not the loss of the `cmd:`-reference freeze. — evidence: target §2 step 3 (l.44-47), step 5 (l.50-51), l.62; plan §1.7 step 4, §0.2 FEA-N3/INT-10; run spec §4.2, §11 "`vars_used` removed"; `internal/fsm/workflow/workflow.go:744-750`.
- [P2] [confidence 50] [advisory] Export deviates from plan §3.5 / SEC-13 ("`llm_call.raw` dropped") without recording it: §5 keeps `llm_call.verdict` and says nothing about `llm_call.error`, but spec 4 r5 puts raw model text (capped at `MaxShort`) into `ParseError` and the kind persists it as `error: "parse: <raw>"` (`kind.go:380-381`). The exported bundle therefore carries up to 1 KB of unredacted judge output per failed parse, in a file the spec routes to durable `docs/metareview/fsm/`. Cross-spec gap between spec 4's persistence decision and spec 3's redaction list. — evidence: target §5 (l.96-103); plan §3.5 (l.406-409), §0.2 SEC-13; spec 4 r5 header "`Raw` capped at `MaxShort` is kept in `ParseError`"; `internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:380-381`; `internal/fsm/judge/judge.go:60-62`.
- [P2] [confidence 50] [advisory] Ledger section needed (answer to the orchestrator's question: yes). Every sibling carries one (run §11, spec 2 §8, spec 4 §9); this spec records deviations only in a prose "r2 changes" header, and the following departures from locked/plan text are recorded nowhere: the two blockers above; `maxAttempts` 3 vs plan §1.9's 0 (behaviourally equal only because `ReadRuns` defaults 0→3, `runchain.go:118-119`); new `status: forked` value; `Diff` `CallSide{Decision *bool, Effort, Error}` replacing plan §3.5's `verdict` field; export adding `workflow.yaml`, sidecars and `audit.torn-*.bin` to plan §3.5's three files; `ERR_COPY_INVALID` replacing plan §1.7.5's `ERR_AUDIT_INVALID`; `tree` rebaseline on every fork vs run spec §7's agent-edit-only wording (spec 2 §8 ledgers its side; this spec should ledger its own). "Shape matches the existing writers (`review.go:22-41`)" is also inexact — that struct has no `attemptNumber`/`maxAttempts`/`mock`/`outcome`/`fsmRunDir` and does have `gitHead`/`reviewers`/`findingIds`/`sourceRefs`; the `taskdone`/`prready` writers are the real precedent. — evidence: target header l.9-19, §4 l.79-80, §5 l.95, §6 l.110-117, §7; plan §1.7 step 5, §1.9, §3.5; run spec §7, §11; `internal/artifactreview/review.go:22-41`; `internal/taskdone/review.go:48`.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] Header and §5 disagree about what the export guarantees: l.13-14 keeps every `node_output` because "the snapshot must re-fold; `output_hash` is checked", but l.99-101 concedes that after redaction `output_hash` no longer matches, the shipped `snapshot.json` is the pre-redaction fold, and the manifest says `chain: "redacted"` — i.e. the exported log cannot be re-folded or hash-verified, so the stated reason for keeping the outputs does not hold. State the actual invariant (exported `audit.jsonl` is evidence, not a foldable log) in one place. — evidence: target l.13-14 vs l.99-101, `Manifest.Chain` l.93.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] Step 8's "every fork" rebaseline makes spec 2's `tree`-when-`TreeHash == ""` path (spec 2 §8, M5 "fork-from-initial fixture") unreachable for real forks: a fork from initial (seq 1, nothing copied) still gets a `tree` from step 8, so the machine's baseline branch is exercised only by a hand-built fixture no producer emits. Not a contradiction, but the two specs should agree on which one owns the baseline so the dead branch is either removed or documented as defence-in-depth. — evidence: target §2 step 8 (l.63-64); spec 2 §8 "`tree` … as a baseline when `TreeHash == ""` (fork-from-initial fixture)", §7 M5.

NOTES: Inside row 3 throughout — no work here falls outside the run spec §12 partition (fork/resume, `Origin`/`Lineage`, freeze rule, git preconditions, `--work-dir`, copy re-validation, diff, export, `fsmRunRecord` + verdict map + ESCALATED), and CLI shapes are correctly deferred to spec 5. Deferred-scope items stay deferred: no `fsm run` (C10), no dashboard (C24/§13), diff/export justified by C24. Cross-spec items I checked and found consistent: child sidecar = child's own bytes (spec 2 §8 obligation); `ForkedAtSeq` = copied+1 (run spec §7); `Origin` shape and provenance range (run spec §3/§4.8); `ERR_COPY_INVALID` name matches spec 4's freeze-policy ledger; `CommonDir` check mirrors spec 2 §5.3 step 3; sdlc-loop judge-swap `--at-iter 0` limitation matches plan §1.7 and spec 5 §6 docs; `Diff` ignoring `reasoning` matches spec 4 §9. `VerifyOrigin`'s "version-selected file" refers to a mechanism the store does not have (single `audit.jsonl`, `Log.Version` fixed at 1, `store.go:77`) — below threshold, mentioned for the author. Findings on `Fork` locking, `attemptNumber` when the parent has no row, and the `llm_call.error` exposure as a security matter are left to the Architecture/Completeness/Security lenses.

### Architecture

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] The `forked` runs.jsonl row poisons the parent's real terminal record. §6 makes `RecordTerminal` "idempotent: a row whose `id` already exists is not written again", and §2 step 9 writes a `forked` row (`status: forked`, `verdict: NEEDS_REVISION`, `outcome: ""`) whenever P has no row. Forking a non-terminal P (judge swap on a run parked at NEEDS_INPUT, or a ctx-cancelled run — §2 step 1 only says a terminal P is "allowed", so live parents are the default) therefore guarantees that when P later reaches `fixed`/`clean`, the machine's `Deps.Terminal` call (machine.go:833, :789, :544) is a no-op and P is durably recorded as NEEDS_REVISION forever; `runchain.ChainTo` (runchain.go:127-131, last-row-wins map) and `escalatedForTarget` (:155) then reason over a wrong verdict, and `metareview status` shows the wrong outcome. Either refuse non-terminal parents (also removes the "P advances while I copy it" race) or key idempotency on `(id, status)` with a `forked` row superseded by a `complete` row (append-only + last-wins is already how `runchain` reads). — evidence: spec §2 step 9 (lines 65-66), §6 (lines 107-108, 116); machine.go:883-888; runchain.go:127-153
- [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] `AcceptWorkflowChange` has no structural-compatibility invariant, and the fold cannot supply one because `run` treats states as opaque. §2 step 1 checks `From` against **P's** workflow only; step 2 then parses arbitrary new bytes and step 7 copies P's events verbatim with their old `State` stamps. If the new workflow renames/removes `From` (or any state in the copied prefix), the child folds cleanly, `Open` succeeds, and the first `Advance` hits `w.NodeFor(state) == nil` → `Outgoing(state)` empty → `TerminalFor` nil → `s.fail(failures[0], head)` indexes an empty slice (machine.go:768-769) — a panic on a fork the spec accepted. Related holes: step 7's "re-validate `node_output` with the child workflow's kind `Decode`" is undefined when the node is gone; `Parse` may yield a different `w.Name` so `RunID(w.Name,…)`/`scope: fsm-<workflow>` diverge from P and `runchain.Resolve` rejects the chain ("ancestor run … does not match", runchain.go:70-73); and `Resolve(effective)` refuses P's stored vars that the new workflow no longer declares (resolve.go:19-23) so a var removal makes the fork impossible rather than dropping the var. Require: `w.Name == P.Workflow`, every state stamped in `[2..seq]` (at least `From`) exists in the child workflow with the same node `Kind`, and P's vars filtered to the new declared set (or `ERR_WORKFLOW_INCOMPATIBLE{reason}`). — evidence: spec §2 steps 1-3, 7 (lines 37-47, 58-60); machine.go:687-771; workflow.go:701; resolve.go:18-23
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] Copy validation is narrower than the fold's rejection set, so several legal-looking forks die mid-copy with a raw store error and a partial child. Step 7 only re-`Decode`s `node_output`; but `Store.Append` runs the full `Apply` on every copied line, and the child's `AllowedCmds` are the **new** set (step 7). A workflow change that removes/renames a `cmd` makes every copied `cmd_call`/`overflow_handler` fail `unsanctioned_cmd` (fold.go:164-171) → `ERR_APPEND_REJECTED` at seq N, not `ERR_COPY_INVALID`; `MaxEvents` (store.go:143-152) can also trip on a copy of a full parent. Fix the shape: build the entire child log in memory (init + copies with `Origin` + `tree` [+ `fix_baseline`]) and `run.FoldFull` it **before** `run.Create`, mapping any `*FoldError` to `ERR_COPY_INVALID{seq, reason}`; then the only remaining crash windows are I/O, and no validation path ever leaves a child directory behind. — evidence: spec §2 step 7 (lines 58-62); fold.go:164-171; store.go:126-152
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] The agent-edit ancestor rule plus "rebaseline at HEAD" silently discards commits made between the checkpoint and the fork. Step 6 allows `IsAncestor(ch, HEAD)` for agent-edit `From`, but step 8 appends `fix_baseline{Head: HEAD}` (the fold forces this: `tree` sets `Snapshot.Head = HEAD`, fold.go:99, and `fix_baseline_head` requires `payload.Head == Snapshot.Head`, fold.go:212-217). So on sdlc-loop a "commit the fix, then `fork --from fix`" recovery re-fails `commit_exists` (`CommitCount(FixEntryHead=HEAD, HEAD) == 0`) exactly like the run it is recovering from; only "fork, then commit" (F2's order) works. Either require `HEAD == ch` for every state (honest semantics: the fork is the fix's starting line) or spell the ordering into the `ERR_TREE_NOT_AT_CHECKPOINT`/resume hint and F2. — evidence: spec §2 steps 6, 8 (lines 52-54, 63-64), §8 F2; fold.go:98-100, 208-218; workflows/sdlc-loop.yaml (`fix → verify, gate: commit_exists`)
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] Incomplete forks are openable and fail later with an unrelated error; the parent `fork` event is a second, weaker source of truth. A crash during step 7, or the `ERR_COPY_INVALID` refusal path, leaves a child whose log is a valid prefix (R10 explicitly accepts `ForkedAtSeq > copies`); `Open` succeeds, and the first `Advance` appends a non-`Origin` event at `seq <= ForkedAtSeq` → `provenance` (fold.go:413-426) → `ERR_AUDIT_INVALID`. A crash between the child's `tree` and step 9 leaves a complete child with no `fork` event on P. Define "complete fork" structurally (`Seq > ForkedAtSeq`, i.e. the rebaseline `tree` is present) and have `Open`/`List` report `ERR_FORK_INCOMPLETE`; state that `init.parent_run_id` is authoritative and P's `fork` event is advisory. — evidence: spec §2 steps 7-9 (lines 58-66); fold.go:413-426; run-persistence spec R10 (line 331)
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] Package placement contradicts `machine`'s charter. `record.go` (direct `.metareview/runs.jsonl` I/O via `internal/state`/`internal/runchain`) and `Export` (writes `<Out>/…`, default `docs/metareview/fsm/<id>/`, copies files) are filesystem effectors inside the package whose doc says "every LLM or shell effect sits behind the interfaces declared here… the machine itself only decides" (types.go:1-4); `Deps.Terminal` exists precisely so `machine` never knows about runs.jsonl (types.go:99). `Export` also has no write seam (`Deps` carries none) and no repo root (`Store.Root()` is `""` for `MemStore`, store_mem.go:23), so its 100%-coverage tests need a real FS. Move `RecordTerminal` and `Export` to `internal/fsm/record` / `internal/fsm/export` (or the CLI package); keep `Fork`/`VerifyOrigin`/`Diff` in `machine` where `load`/`session` live. — evidence: spec §1 (lines 24-29), §5 (lines 92-104); types.go:1-5, 85-100; store_mem.go:23
- [P2] [confidence 50] [advisory] The `runchain` escalation claim in §6 is unowned and, as worded, self-defeating. "`runchain.Resolve` applies the same-target escalation rule to forks of an escalated run" — but no step of §2 calls `Resolve`, and `attemptNumber = 1 + parent's` presupposes a parent row that a nil `Deps.Terminal` or a crash never wrote. If `Resolve` **is** meant to gate `Fork`, then forking an `overflow` parent (verdict ESCALATED) at the same `headSha` is refused (`previous run … already escalated`, runchain.go:75-80) — which kills the primary recovery for overflow (raise budget / swap judge). State explicitly who calls `Resolve`, with which `HeadSHA`, and whether `overflow` parents are forkable. — evidence: spec §6 (lines 115-117), §8 F9; runchain.go:64-91
- [P2] [confidence 50] [advisory] The exported `audit.jsonl` is neither verifiable nor foldable, and the spec says both things about it. r2 preamble: "keeps every `node_output` (the snapshot must re-fold; `output_hash` is checked)"; §5 body: redaction changes outputs "so `output_hash` no longer matches" and `chain: "redacted"`. Nothing says whether `prev` is recomputed over the redacted lines: if left intact, `parseLines` fails at the first redacted line (`prev`, store.go:89-91) and nothing after it is checkable; if re-chained, the manifest's `chain` claim means nothing about origin. Pick one: keep original `prev`/`Origin` bytes and add a per-line `redacted: true` marker so unredacted lines still verify hash-to-hash against the original, or re-chain and drop the verification claim. Also `audit.torn-*.bin` is copied raw, bypassing every redaction rule (Security's call, but it contradicts the section's stated goal). — evidence: spec lines 13-14 vs 95-103; store.go:76-97
- [P3] [confidence 75] [advisory] `From` may name a terminal state (`done`, `failed`): the transition into it exists, so step 1 finds a checkpoint, the copied transition carries `Outcome`, the child is born terminal, `tree` is post-terminal-legal (fold.go:333-339) and step 8 succeeds; the first `Advance` writes a runs.jsonl row and returns `ERR_RUN_TERMINAL`. Refuse `w.IsTerminal(From)` with `ERR_CHECKPOINT_NOT_FOUND{reason: terminal_state}`. — evidence: spec §2 step 1 (lines 37-40); fold.go:333-339; machine.go:540-548
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] `Diff`'s `CallSide.Decision` = "the kind's decision field (`match`/`is_real`/`still_present`)" hard-codes spec 4's verdict vocabulary as magic strings inside `machine`, which otherwise "imports nothing from `kind`"; adding a kind means editing `diff.go`. Have `NodeKind`/`Registry` expose the decision accessor (or pass a `func(kind string, verdict json.RawMessage) *bool`). — evidence: spec §4 (lines 83-84); types.go:44-64
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] `VerifyOrigin` "for each `Origin`-bearing event, read the parent's `EventsWithLines`" reads and re-parses the whole parent file per copied event (up to `ForkedAtSeq` times); every `Origin` names the immediate parent, so read once and index by seq. Same for `Fork` step 7: `load` discards the lines (`log, _, err :=`, machine.go:310), so `Fork` must call `EventsWithLines` itself under P's lock — say so, and say P's lock is held for steps 1-9 so the `fork` stamps and the checkpoint come from the same fold. — evidence: spec §3 (lines 72-74), §2 (line 36); machine.go:297-313

NOTES: Fold walk-through of the happy path checks out: copies `2..seq` keep stamps and indexes consistent because the prefix is byte-identical (`indexes[k]` continuity, fold.go:145-154); the copied agent-edit transition correctly leaves `FixEntryHead` empty (fold.go:202) and `tree`→`fix_baseline` restores it under `fix_baseline_kind/head/order`; `mock_stamp` exempts `Origin` events; `cmd_call` ordinals include copies via `load`'s count (machine.go:384-392) as spec 2 line 266 requires; `Origin.Version = Log.Version` is always 1 today (store.go:77). Two smaller semantics worth a sentence each: `--from <initial>` without `--at-iter` yields seq 1 (restart) rather than "latest" on a looped run, contradicting the "latest otherwise" default; and a dirty non-agent-edit tree at fork is baked into the child's `TreeHash` baseline, so in `enforcing` mode a fork launders an unsanctioned edit (Security lens should weigh it). `RecordTerminal` concurrency is the pre-existing `AppendJSONL` convention (single `O_APPEND` write, state.go:17-32) and is fine under the run lock.

### Intent preservation

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 100] [blocking] Export's "leaks no secrets" intent is inverted by `snapshot.json`: §5 (L100-101) writes the **pre-redaction** fold, but `run.Snapshot` carries every field the audit redaction removes — `Vars` (raw values, so `IncludeVars=false` is a no-op), `LastError.Detail` (the `commit_exists` failure embeds the full working diff up to 64 KB), `TreeStatus` (full porcelain), absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, and `NodeOutputs` with `Rejected[].desc` intact. Plan §3.5/SEC-13 says the export *redacts*; r2 ships an unredacted twin beside the redacted log in `docs/metareview/fsm/<id>/` (a committed path). The `output_hash` re-fold argument justifies redacting the snapshot too or omitting it, not shipping it raw. — evidence: target §5 L96-104; `internal/fsm/run/types.go` `Snapshot{Vars, RepoRoot, WorkDir, TreeStatus, NodeOutputs, LastError}`; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:173-180` (gate failure → `LastError` with Detail); `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go:114-122` (detail = status + working diff); plan §3.5, §0.2 SEC-13.
- [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] The GATE_FAILED resume recipe is inverted for the ordering the design and plan prescribe ("fix the commit, resume"). Step 6 adopts the FEA-N4 ancestor rule for `agent-edit` precisely so "commits on top are the node's own work", but step 8 then appends `tree{Head: HEAD}` + `fix_baseline{Head: HEAD}`, which the fold makes the new `FixEntryHead`; `commit_exists` counts `FixEntryHead..HEAD`, so an operator who commits *before* forking (the plan §1.7 step 6 order: "commit, `advance`, `record`, `advance`") gets `ERR_NO_COMMIT` again with no way out short of an extra commit. F2 only tests the fork-then-commit order, so the spec is self-consistent but silently drops the commit-first path the ancestor rule exists to serve. Either baseline at the checkpoint head `ch` (what the rule's rationale implies) or state that commits must come *after* the fork and drop the ancestor allowance. — evidence: target §2 step 6 L52-54, step 8 L63-64, F2 L129; plan §1.7 step 6 and §0.2 FEA-N4; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:99` (tree updates Head), `:216-218` (fix_baseline sets FixEntryHead); `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go:106-114`.
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] "Same-target escalation" is asserted, not delivered. §6 says `runchain.Resolve` applies the rule to forks of an escalated run, but the `Fork` algorithm (§2 steps 1-10) never calls it and step 1 explicitly allows a terminal parent; even if spec 5 wires it, `escalatedForTarget` exempts records whose `headSha` differs from the caller's, and a fork at any earlier checkpoint has a different HEAD than the parent's terminal `headSha`, so an unchanged-target retry (`--from fix --at-iter 2`, no vars) of an `overflow` parent passes as a "changed head" and resets the chain. Also, r2 changes plan §1.9 `maxAttempts: 0` to 3 with `attemptNumber = 1 + parent's`, which makes a judge-swap experiment chain (a `--var` change, not a retry) count toward escalation without saying who converts attempt 4 into ESCALATED. Say explicitly which forks are "retries" and where the rule is enforced. — evidence: target §2 L39-40, §6 L115-117; plan §1.9; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:155-165`, `:167-192`.
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] `Diff`'s cell-by-cell verdict comparison degrades for the headline use case (§17 adjudicator validation). (a) `Same` requires `Confidence` equal, so two judges that agree on every `is_real` decision still show every row as `Same: false`; the discriminating signal (decision flips) is buried. (b) Rows align by `(node, iter, index)`, but in `match-then-adjudicate` the adjudicate ordinals depend on which candidates the *match* calls marked seen — a different judge produces a different seen set, so adjudicate rows after the first divergence pair unrelated candidates (InputHash mismatch makes it visible but not readable). Align adjudicate rows by `(node, iter, kind, input_hash)` and separate `DecisionSame` from `ConfidenceSame`. — evidence: target §4 L84-86; spec 4 §4.2 steps 1-2 ("indexes continue" over never-seen candidates); design §10 "diffed cell-by-cell", §14.3.
- [P2] [confidence 50] [advisory] Export redaction removes exactly the data the §17 audit needs: `Rejected[].desc` is emptied, so a reader of the export cannot see *what* the judge called a hallucination (only ID + confidence), and `review-lenses` outputs lose `severity`/`category`/`source`, which are not sensitive but are the only record of which lens produced a finding. Neither removal prevents a secret leak; both reduce auditability. The spec 4 handoff only says `Rejected` is "redacted on export", not that the audit becomes unusable for the validation it exists for. — evidence: target §5 L98-100; spec 4 §9 ledger ("Rejected persisted in node_output only"); design §14.3, §17.
- [P2] [confidence 50] [advisory] The `forked` runs.jsonl row can permanently mask a parent's real verdict: step 9 writes `status: forked, verdict: NEEDS_REVISION` when the parent has no row, and §6 makes `RecordTerminal` idempotent by id; nothing in the machine or fold makes a forked parent read-only (`fork` is a no-op event), so a parent forked mid-run and then advanced to `fixed` never records PASS. Either upsert by id (last status wins) or refuse `Advance` on a forked parent. — evidence: target §2 step 9 L65-66, §6 L107-108; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:101,335` (`ForkData` no-op); spec 2 §5.4 step 2 (Terminal called on every terminal advance, dedup by id).
- [P3] [confidence 50] [follow-up] `record.data` is kept verbatim on export while everything else agent-supplied is capped or reduced; `AllowedCmd.FileHashes` keys and pinned `argv[0]` remain absolute paths although `repo_root`/`work_dir` are relativised. Minor leak surface relative to the stated "no secrets" goal. — evidence: target §5 L101-103; `internal/fsm/run/types.go` `AllowedCmd{Argv, FileHashes}`.

NOTES: The wording rule is respected (no "deterministic result(s)" in the target; grep returned no match). The judge-swap recipe itself is sound: the checkpoint is the transition *into* `adjudicate`, so no `llm_call` for that key is copied, discover's `node_output`/`delta_applied` replay without re-spend, the freeze rule over `NodesRun` correctly refuses `JUDGE` on sdlc-loop at iter ≥ 1 (verify referenced it) and accepts it on review-loop or at iter 0, and F3 pins the re-run to adjudicate only via `MockJudge.Calls()`. The `overflow → ESCALATED` mapping is a defensible reading of C12/C27 even though plan §1.6 said NEEDS_REVISION; the asymmetry with `stalled` (the outcome that most literally means "same-target retry is futile") staying NEEDS_REVISION is worth one sentence of rationale. Blocking findings are the two inversions: the unredacted `snapshot.json` in an export whose purpose is redaction, and `fix_baseline{HEAD}` defeating the commit-first resume the ancestor rule was adopted to allow.

### Security

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 90] [blocking] `snapshot.json` nullifies every export redaction. §5 says the exported `snapshot.json` is "the **pre-redaction** fold written by the exporter" and F8 asserts "snapshot equals the pre-redaction fold". `run.Snapshot` carries, in plaintext, exactly the fields §5 redacts in the events: `Vars` (the var values `init.vars` is hashed to hide), `AllowedCmds[].Argv`, absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, full `TreeStatus` (porcelain), `LastError.Detail` (for `commit_exists` this is the porcelain status plus the working diff), and every `NodeOutputs` value (rejected descs, full lens findings). Anyone who reads the committed `docs/metareview/fsm/<run-id>/snapshot.json` gets everything `audit.jsonl` was scrubbed of. Fix: define a snapshot redaction that mirrors the event redaction (Vars→`sha256:` unless `IncludeVars`, argv-from-vars, paths relative, `TreeStatus`→file list, `LastError.Detail`→summary, per-kind `NodeOutputs` trimming) and drop the "pre-redaction" wording; the manifest already declares `chain: "redacted"` so the hash mismatch is accepted. — evidence: spec §5 (lines 96-97, F8 row); `internal/fsm/run/types.go:179` (`Vars`), `:184` (`AllowedCmds`), `:186-187` (`RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`), `:197` (`TreeStatus`), `:208` (`NodeOutputs`), `:212` (`LastError`); `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:99` (status folded), `:179` (LastError folded); `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go` `commitExists` detail = `--- status ---\n%s--- working diff ---\n%s`.
- [P1] [confidence 85] [blocking] Argv-from-vars redaction is incomplete and trivially bypassed. §5 replaces only `init.allowed_cmds[].argv` elements *equal to* a var value. The identical resolved argv is persisted verbatim in every `cmd_call.argv` and in `overflow_handler.argv` (neither is in the redaction list), the `init.allowed_cmds[].file_hashes` map keys embed argv elements as paths, and any element that *contains* the value (`--token=$TOKEN`, `Bearer $TOKEN`, `https://x:$TOKEN@host`) is untouched because substitution is substring-based. Fix: redact by substring replacement of every var value (with a minimum length) across `init.allowed_cmds[].argv`, `file_hashes` keys, `cmd_call.argv`, `overflow_handler.argv`, and the snapshot's `AllowedCmds`; or adopt spec 4's ledger stance ("vars are not secrets", judge-kinds §ledger line 295) explicitly and drop var hashing — the two specs currently disagree. — evidence: spec §5 "`init.allowed_cmds[].argv` elements equal to a var value replaced likewise"; `internal/fsm/cmdexec/cmdexec.go` `exec()`: `spec := Spec{... Argv: append([]string(nil), c.Argv...)}` then `data := run.CmdCallData{Name: name, Argv: spec.Argv, ...}`; `internal/fsm/machine/machine.go:855-862` (`argv = c.Argv` into `OverflowHandlerData`); `internal/fsm/workflow/resolve.go` `Resolve`: `dd.Argv[i] = sub(a)` (substring substitution) and `ResolveCmds`: `fh[p] = h` with `p = candidatePath(workDir, a)`.
- [P2] [confidence 75] [blocking] The `forked` runs.jsonl row permanently suppresses a parent's real verdict, defeating the ESCALATED guardrail. §6 makes `RecordTerminal` "idempotent: a row whose `id` already exists is not written again" and step 9 writes a `forked` row (`verdict: NEEDS_REVISION`) for a parent that has none; §2 step 1 allows forking a non-terminal parent and nothing stops that parent from advancing afterwards (`fork` folds as no-op). So: fork mid-run (an agent-satisfiable action), let the parent run on to `overflow` → `Deps.Terminal(P)` is skipped → no `ESCALATED` row → `runchain` same-target escalation never fires and retries continue. Fix: idempotency keyed on `(id, status)` (append a superseding `complete` row; `runchain.ChainTo` already takes the last row per id) or write the `forked` row only for terminal parents. — evidence: spec §6; spec §2 step 1 ("A terminal P is allowed") and step 9; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:101` (`*ForkData` → no change), `:335` (`fork` post-terminal allowed, not required); `internal/runchain/runchain.go:126-131` (`byID[record.ID] = record`, last wins); `internal/state/state.go:17-31` (`AppendJSONL` append-only).
- [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] Fork never states that it consults `runchain.Resolve` or refuses an ESCALATED parent/target, and an accepted workflow can rename the target identity. §6 only says `runchain.Resolve` "applies the same-target escalation rule to forks of an escalated run" with no call site; §2 step 1 explicitly permits forking a terminal (incl. `overflow`) parent. With `AcceptWorkflowChange`, the child id, `scope: fsm-<workflow>` and `target.id: <workflow>@<base>` derive from the *new* `w.Name`, so a renamed workflow either breaks `ChainTo` (ancestor scope/target mismatch → error) or, if `Resolve` is not called, produces a fresh target that evades escalation. Require: a step 0 `runchain.Resolve{Scope/Target from P, PreviousRunID: P, HeadSHA}` that refuses escalated chains, and `w.Name == P.Workflow` on workflow change (or scope/target always taken from the parent). — evidence: spec §2 step 1, step 7 (`run.RunID(w.Name, …)`), §6; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:64-80` (`ChainTo` + ancestor scope/target check + "previous run already escalated").
- [P2] [confidence 80] [advisory] `Export` path handling is unspecified where the store is careful. Default `Out` is *inside the repo tree* (`docs/metareview/fsm/<run-id>/`), so repo-controlled content (a checked-in symlink at `docs/metareview/fsm`, or a pre-existing file at a copied sidecar name) determines where the exporter writes; `--out` is arbitrary. The spec says nothing about mkdir perms, refusing symlinked path components, `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` creation, refusing a non-empty target dir, measuring `MaxBytes` before the first write, or cleaning partial output on `ERR_EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`. Copied names are pattern-safe (`ValidateRunID`, `ValidSidecarName`, `audit.torn-*`), which is good; the destination is the gap. — evidence: spec §5 ("Default `Out` = …", "Over `MaxBytes` → `ERR_EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`"); contrast `internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go` `checkComponents` (refuses symlinked components) and `openNoFollow(..., O_CREATE|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY, 0o600)`; `internal/fsm/machine/sidecar.go:19,23` and `Write` (`O_EXCL|syscall.O_NOFOLLOW`).
- [P3] [confidence 70] [advisory] `VerifyOrigin` proves the parent line still exists, not that the copy is faithful: it compares `Origin.Hash` with the parent's current `LineHash` and never compares the child's copied `Data`/`At`/`State`/`Iter`/`Node`/`Mock` to the parent event; `Fold`'s provenance rule is shape-only. A child whose copied prefix was edited (Origin left intact, chain re-stamped) reports every check `ok`. Add a `content_mismatch` reason comparing the machine-set fields. — evidence: spec §3; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:412-425` (`checkProvenance` checks only `RunID`/`Seq`/`Version>=1`/`Hash!=""`).
- [P3] [confidence 75] [advisory] Redaction list drifts from spec 2 §12 row 3 and omits carriers. Spec 2's ledger assigns spec 3 to redact `llm_call.Verdict`, `record.data`, `node_output`; r2 keeps all three. Unclassified fields: `converge.reason` (command-controlled stdout `{stop, reason}` up to `MaxText`), `warn.detail` (`MaxText`; the overflow-handler failure detail embeds `err.Error()` incl. absolute paths), `delta_applied.findings/confirmed` (keeps `severity`/`category`/`source`/`desc` that the `node_output` trimming removes), `init.goldens[].comment`, `llm_call.error` (spec 4 says `ParseError` carries `raw:` capped at `MaxShort`), and `gate.error.detail` for non-`commit_exists` gates (executor/node-output error text) being forced into a `{files, insertions, deletions}` shape. Also `workflow.yaml` is copied verbatim, so var *defaults* leak even when `init.vars` is hashed. Enumerate keep/redact per field per event type and reconcile with spec 2 §12. — evidence: spec 2 §12 row 3 (`docs/specs/2026-08-26-metareview-0.9.0-fsm-run-persistence.md:379`); spec 3 §5; `internal/fsm/converge/converge.go:268-275`; `internal/fsm/machine/machine.go:874-877`; `internal/fsm/run/event.go` (`WarnData.Detail`, `ConvergeData.Reason`, `LLMCallData.Error`).
- [P3] [confidence 65] [advisory] Workflow-change path is under-specified: step 2 parses `WorkflowBytes` without the `MaxWorkflowBytes` cap `Init` applies (sidecar reads are capped at `MaxPayload`, direct bytes are not); step 1 checks `From` against P's workflow only, and re-validation covers only `node_output` `Decode` — behavior when the accepted workflow lacks a node at a copied state or lacks `From` is unstated; the freeze rule is computed on P's resolved workflow, so an accepted workflow can change a ran state's `model:` literal with no `ERR_VAR_FROZEN` (acceptable under consent, but say so and make `Diff.SameWorkflow=false` surface it). — evidence: spec §2 steps 1-3, 7; `internal/fsm/machine/machine.go:74-76` (`MaxWorkflowBytes` check in `Init`); `internal/fsm/machine/sidecar.go` `Read` (`LimitReader(run.MaxPayload+1)`).
- [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] "`repo_root`/`work_dir` made relative to the repo root" is undefined for a `--work-dir` worktree outside the repo root (`git worktree add ../x` is the documented recovery path in step 6's hint) — it yields `../x` or the absolute path; and unsalted `sha256:` of var values is offline-guessable for low-entropy values. State both. — evidence: spec §2 step 6 hint, §5.

NOTES: Checked and found sound: re-consent on fork (step 5 recomputes `ResolveCmds` in the child's `WorkDir`, sha compared to `P.CmdsSHA256`, `VerifyCmds` re-hashes at exec time — `cmdexec.go` `exec()`); repo mode cannot be loosened (child init inherits `RepoMode`, and `load` overrides the parsed workflow's `repo_mode` with the snapshot's — `machine.go:359`); mock mode is inherited and the registry must agree (`machine.go:369-380`); `--work-dir` is absolute + `CommonDir` equality (same as `Init`, `machine.go:92-115`); hosts cannot append `tree`/`transition`/`fork`/`Origin`-bearing events via `Record` (allowlist `machine.go` `Record`, `isEventType` refusal); run ids and sidecar names are pattern-validated so copied names cannot traverse. The freeze basis (`NodesRun`) is populated by every node's `node_output` (`fold.go:109-111`, `machine.go` `runNode`), so the var-freeze rule itself holds. The two P1s and the P2 idempotency finding are exploitable gaps in stated guardrails (export redaction; ESCALATED stops same-target retries); the rest is precision the implementer will otherwise have to guess.

### Testing-quality

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [90] [blocking] §5/F8 redaction is self-defeating and the fixture as written ratifies the leak. `snapshot.json` is "the **pre-redaction** fold written by the exporter", but `run.Snapshot` (internal/fsm/run/types.go:190-235) carries `Vars` (plain values), `TreeStatus` (full porcelain), `AllowedCmds[].Argv`, absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, `NodeOutputs map[string]json.RawMessage` (the raw `match-then-adjudicate` output incl. `rejected[].desc`, the raw `review-lenses` output with every field), and `Confirmed`/`AllFound` `Desc`. Every marker F8 seeds (vars, argv-from-vars, paths, tree status, rejected descs, review-lenses fields) reappears verbatim in `snapshot.json`. So the F8 "seeded markers" sweep either (a) is scoped to `audit.jsonl` and the leak ships green, or (b) sweeps `<Out>/` and fails by design, at which point the implementer scopes it down — and F8's last clause "snapshot equals the pre-redaction fold" actively asserts the leaking file. Fix: apply the same redaction to the exported snapshot (or export a projection) and require F8 to assert every marker is absent from **every byte under `<Out>/`** (manifest.json included), plus a positive assertion that the redacted snapshot still has the kept fields — evidence: spec §5 lines 97-104 ("snapshot.json is the pre-redaction fold"), §8 F8; types.go:200 `Vars`, :216 `TreeStatus`, :226 `NodeOutputs`, :220-221 `Confirmed`/`AllFound`.
- [P2] [75] [blocking] §6 idempotency + §2 step 9 make the verdict map unreachable for any parent forked before it terminated, and F9 cannot see it. The `forked` row (`status: forked`, `verdict: NEEDS_REVISION`, `outcome: ""`) is written under P's id; when P is later resumed to a terminal (e.g. `overflow`), `Deps.Terminal(P)` finds the id "already exists" and writes nothing — P's real verdict (`ESCALATED`) never lands and `runchain.escalatedForTarget` never sees it. F9's "idempotent on a second call" only checks the same view twice, so an implementation that ignores (per spec) and one that upserts both pass. Add the fixture: fork a non-terminal P, resume P to `overflow`, assert P's row `status`/`verdict`/`outcome`; and decide in §6 whether `forked` → terminal is an upsert (then idempotency is "by id **and** status", not by id) — evidence: §2 step 9, §6 ("Idempotent: a row whose `id` already exists is not written again", "A `forked` row … `verdict: NEEDS_REVISION`"), F9.
- [P2] [75] [advisory] §2 step 1 checkpoint selection is ambiguous for the initial state on a looped run and F1 cannot discriminate. "latest otherwise; if `From == P.Initial` and (`AtIter` nil or 0) → `seq = 1`" reads two ways when a 2-iteration run has a `transition` with `To == discover, Iter == 1` (loop transitions are stamped with the post-increment iter, machine.go:803-805 / fold.go:69-78): `--from discover` (nil) → seq 1, or → the iter-1 re-entry. F1 forks "a happy sdlc run" (one iteration) and F3 only asserts an error code at iter 1. Add a 2-iteration fixture asserting `ForkedAtSeq` for `--from discover` (nil), `--at-iter 0`, `--at-iter 1`, and `--from adjudicate` (nil) = the iter-1 transition, plus `--at-iter 2` → `ERR_CHECKPOINT_NOT_FOUND{at_iter: 2}` — evidence: §2 step 1, §8 F1/F3.
- [P2] [75] [advisory] §4/F7 `Diff` lacks the fixtures that fail on plausible wrong implementations: (1) no per-kind `Decision` case — an implementation that only reads `match` returns nil for `is_real`/`still_present` verdicts and reports two adjudicate calls with opposite verdicts as `Same`; (2) no `null`-verdict / absent-bool case (nil vs `false` must not be `Same`; nil vs nil should be); (3) no `CommonPrefixSeq` assertion for parent-vs-child (`== ForkedAtSeq`) — comparing stored lines instead of `Data` sees `Origin`/`Seq` and yields 0 or 1; (4) no `SameWorkflow == false` after `AcceptWorkflowChange`; (5) `TransRow.Same` is undefined in §4 so its fixture cannot be discriminating; (6) as specified, an errored call and a null-verdict call with equal `InputHash` compare `Same` (`Error` is not in the predicate) — say so or include `Error`. "Same ignores reasoning" is tautological given `CallSide` has no reasoning field (fine as a regression guard) — evidence: §4 `CallSide`/`Same` definition, F7.
- [P2] [75] [advisory] F9 "shape vs existing writers (decode with `runchain.ReadRuns`)" cannot fail on a wrong shape: `runchain.Record` (internal/runchain/runchain.go:31-46) ignores unknown keys and zero-fills missing ones, so a row missing `target.type`, `executionMode`, `fsmRunDir`, `mock`, `outcome`, or with the wrong `scope` prefix decodes cleanly. Also "matches the existing writers" is not literally true: `internal/artifactreview/review.go:22-41` has `previousRunId *string` (JSON `null` for a root), `reviewers`, `findingIds`, `sourceRefs`, `gitHead`, none in §6's shape, and §6 adds `attemptNumber`/`maxAttempts`/`mock`/`outcome`/`fsmRunDir`. Fixture: a golden JSON line per outcome, decoded with `DisallowUnknownFields` into a struct listing exactly §6's keys, and a `previousRunId` value for a root run (`""` vs `null`) pinned — evidence: §6 shape block, F9.
- [P2] [50] [advisory] "`runchain.Resolve` applies the same-target escalation rule to forks of an escalated run" (§6) has no owner in §2 — no step calls `Resolve`, and F9's "`--previous-run` resolution" is a CLI flag (spec 5). An implementation that forks an `ESCALATED` parent freely passes every row. Either add a step (parent row `ESCALATED` → refuse, with the fixture) or explicitly hand it to spec 5. Note `escalatedForTarget` skips records whose `headSha` differs (runchain.go:155-165), so a child at a new HEAD would not be blocked anyway; `target.id` uses `base_sha`, so decide what "same target" means for forks. Borderline Completeness — evidence: §6 last paragraph, F9, runchain.go:155-165.
- [P2] [75] [advisory] No refusal-path postconditions and no failure sweeps, though the house style and the exact-100% gate demand them. §8 has no row asserting that after every refusal (steps 1-6, and `ERR_COPY_INVALID` at step 7) P is byte-identical and no child exists (steps 1-6) — a wrong implementation that appends `fork` to P before copying passes F1. No row exercises `Sidecar.Write`, `run.Create`, child/parent `Lock`, per-copied-event `Append`, `tree`/`fix_baseline`/`fork` append, `Git.Head/CommonDir/WorkTree/Status/IsAncestor` errors, `Terminal` error at step 9 (fork event already on P — is a retry a second child?), `Export` FS/mkdir/write failures, or `RecordTerminal` read/write failures. The seams exist (`countingStore.failAt/failType/failOp/failEvAt`, `failingGit`, `h.termErr`, helpers_test.go chmod/badFile), but without spec'd postconditions those tests will mirror whatever the implementation does. Add an F10 row: "each failure seam × expected error passthrough × P untouched / child dir left" — evidence: §8 (no such row), harness_test.go:213-252, machine_test.go:179-201, 1123-1144, 1319-1338 (the existing sweeps).
- [P3] [50] [advisory] F4 "child `TreeHash` == its worktree's" only discriminates if the worktree fake reports a different `Tree`/`Status`/`Head` than the parent dir (`gitFakes.byDir`); with the shared `def` fake, computing the rebaseline from P's `WorkDir` passes. Specify distinct values and assert `tree.Head == child HEAD` (≠ `ch` in the agent-edit ancestor case) and `fix_baseline.Head` likewise — evidence: §2 step 8, F4, harness_test.go:155-165.
- [P3] [50] [advisory] Rules named in the preamble/§2 with no row: ordinal continuity (copied `cmd_call`s counted by `RunnerDeps.CmdCalls` — assert via `h.runner.ordinal` on the child), `ValidateRunID`, `ERR_RUN_NOT_FOUND{detail}` passthrough for `Fork`/`Export`/`VerifyOrigin`, `VerifyOrigin` "version-selected file" (only v1 exists — say it is untestable), `Export` `MaxBytes` refusal leaving no partial `<Out>/`, `Manifest.Redacted` contents (unspecified → not discriminable), `init.allowed_cmds[].file_hashes` keys carrying a var value (redaction covers `argv` only; the harness `FileHash` fake only hashes `/bin/bash`, so a marker sweep would not catch it), mock scenario re-verification on fork (`MockLoad`) — evidence: preamble r2 list, §2 step 7, §3, §5, machine.go:393.

NOTES: The freeze rule's "resolved vs parsed" question is moot in the current code — `Resolve` shallow-copies the workflow (`c := *w`, resolve.go:58) so `Refs`/`CmdRefs` and therefore `VarsReferencedBy` are identical on both; F3's iter-0-ok / iter-1-frozen pair does discriminate "prefix fold `NodesRun`" from "full fold `NodesRun`" only if the F3 parent has already run adjudicate — state that the parent is a completed run. The Origin-hash fixture (F1 "hashes match `EventsWithLines`") is properly discriminating, and `fold.go:415-425` already rejects a rebaseline `tree` that carries `Origin` and copied events whose `Seq` ≠ `Origin.Seq`, so "child folds" covers those cases for free. Re-Decode (F5) is discriminating given the `fakeKind.decode` hook. Coverage: every seam needed for 100% on `fork.go`/`diff.go`/`record.go` is declared; `export.go` writes to the real FS and reads `audit.torn-*.bin` outside the `Sidecar` interface, so its error paths need the jsonl store in a temp dir plus chmod — feasible with helpers_test.go but should be stated in §8.

### Data-migration

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 90] [blocking] The runs.jsonl recorder is idempotent by `id` alone, and step 9 writes a `forked` row (`status: forked`, `verdict: NEEDS_REVISION`, `outcome: ""`) for any parent that has no row yet — including a **non-terminal** parent (paused at `needs_input`, crashed, torn, or an earlier `--at-iter` checkpoint on a live run). When that parent later reaches terminal, `Deps.Terminal(P)` finds the id and skips, so the parent's real verdict (`fixed`→PASS, `overflow`→ESCALATED) is never persisted: the `forked` row silently stands as the run's verdict forever, and `runchain.escalatedForTarget` never sees the escalation. Existing readers already tolerate a superseding row with the same id (last-write-wins `byID` maps), so the fix is a `(id, status)` idempotency key or "a `complete` row supersedes a `forked` row", plus stating that `forked` is non-terminal. — evidence: spec §2 step 9 (lines 65-66), §6 lines 107-108, 115-116; spec 2 §5.4 step 2 ("idempotent — spec 3 dedups by run id"); `internal/runchain/runchain.go:127-130` and `internal/reviewlog/reviewlog.go:241-244` (`byID[...] = record`, last wins); `internal/fsm/machine/machine.go:884-887`.
- [P2] [confidence 70] [blocking] `AcceptWorkflowChange` copies the prefix verbatim under a **different** sidecar: copied `transition.{from,to,to_kind}`, `state`/`node` stamps and `llm_call.kind` name P's workflow, but only `node_output` payloads are re-`Decode`d, and `From` is validated against P's workflow, not the child's. If the new workflow renames/removes a prefix state or changes a state's kind, the child's fold `StateKind` (from the copied `to_kind`) and `w.NodeFor(state)` from its own sidecar disagree — two truths for one persisted run (tree-cadence agent-edit exemption, `fix_baseline_kind`, node-output matching all read one or the other). Require a prefix-compatibility check (every state in the copied prefix incl. `From` exists in the new workflow with the same node name and kind; `initial` unchanged) → else `ERR_WORKFLOW_CHANGED{reason: prefix_incompatible}`. — evidence: spec §2 step 1 line 39 ("state of P's workflow"), step 2 lines 41-43, step 7 lines 58-60; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:202` (4.6 `ToKind`→`StateKind`), `internal/fsm/machine/machine.go:563-571`, `:923-924`.
- [P1] [confidence 85] [advisory] `snapshot.json` is the **pre-redaction** fold, which contains every value the audit redaction removes: `Snapshot.Vars` values, raw `NodeOutputs` (incl. `rejected[].desc` and full review-lenses findings), `TreeStatus`, `LastError.Detail`, absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, `AllowedCmds.Argv`. F8 even asserts "snapshot equals the pre-redaction fold", testing the leak in. Export the redacted fold (apply the same field redactions to the Snapshot) or drop `snapshot.json` and record the `output_hash` divergence in the manifest. Not corruption, so advisory under this lens's blocking rule, but Security should treat it as blocking. — evidence: spec §5 lines 100-101, F8 line 135; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:200,205,207-208,217,226,229`.
- [P2] [confidence 80] [advisory] `audit.torn-*.bin` is copied verbatim into the export — a raw, never-redacted partial line (could be `cmd_call.stdout`, `gate.error.detail`, a torn `init.vars`). Also `Sidecar.Read/List` refuse `audit.*` names, so the exporter must bypass the seam with direct FS reads, which the spec does not say. Prefer recording `{name, sha256, bytes}` in the manifest, or copy only under an explicit opt-in. — evidence: spec §5 line 96; `internal/fsm/machine/sidecar.go:23,105-122`; `internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go:229`.
- [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] Fork is not atomic across parent and child: the child is fully created (steps 7-8) before P is locked and appended (step 9). Step 9 can fail (`ERR_RUN_LOCKED` — P's lock is first taken there; `ERR_AUDIT_TORN` — a torn P is readable at step 1 but unappendable), leaving a valid child with no `fork` event in P and no `forked` row. `ERR_COPY_INVALID` likewise leaves a child dir that `List()` shows as an ordinary run and that `Open` loads cleanly (sidecar present, chain valid); its inertness relies only on `checkProvenance` rejecting the next non-Origin append at seq ≤ `ForkedAtSeq`. Lock P and require `Torn == nil` before `run.Create`; on `ERR_COPY_INVALID` remove the child dir or state that such a directory is a recognised orphan. — evidence: spec §2 steps 7-9 (lines 55-66); `internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go:194-205,276-305`; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:414-425`.
- [P2] [confidence 70] [follow-up] `Origin.Version` / "version-selected file": `RunStore` has no versioned read (`EventsWithLines(runID)` only; `parseLines` hard-codes `Version: 1`). Under run §5.4's `Migrate` contract `EventsWithLines` targets the highest-version file, so after a parent migration every `Origin{Version: 1}` hash is checked against v2 lines and the whole prefix reports `hash_mismatch`. Either add `EventsWithLines(runID, version)` to the store contract now or specify the fallback reason (`version_unavailable`, not `hash_mismatch`). — evidence: spec §3 lines 72-74; `internal/fsm/run/store.go:51-60,77`; run spec §5.4.
- [P2] [confidence 65] [advisory] runchain semantics for fork rows: `scope`/`target.id` derive from the **child's** workflow name, so a renamed workflow under `AcceptWorkflowChange` makes `runchain.Resolve` reject the chain ("ancestor run does not match"); and a fork of an `overflow` parent — explicitly allowed by §2 step 1 — yields rows for which `Resolve(PreviousRunID=child)` fails with "same target already escalated in run P" at the same head, orphaning every grandchild from `--previous-run`. State whether `Fork` consults `Resolve` (and refuses) or whether fsm chains bypass the same-target rule (then §6 line 117 is wrong). — evidence: spec §2 line 39-40, §6 lines 110, 116-117, F9; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:70-79,88-90`.
- [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] Row-shape compatibility is asserted ("shape matches the existing writers") but the row adds `mock` (bool, while the run's mock identity is the string `rel#hash`), `outcome`, `fsmRunDir`, and writes `previousRunId` as a string where artifactreview writes `*string` null and taskdone omits it; `createdAt`/`updatedAt` format is unspecified. All current readers decode by struct so this is compatible today, but the spec should say so: schemaVersion stays 1, new keys are additive, `status: forked` is a new vocabulary value readers must treat as non-terminal. — evidence: spec §6 lines 110-113; `internal/artifactreview/review.go:29,109-113`; `internal/taskdone/review.go:46`; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:32-47`; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:202`.
- [P3] [confidence 55] [advisory] Redaction-stability details left open: `init.vars` "replaced by `sha256:`" — literal or `sha256:<hex>` (spec 2's `Payload` uses the hex form; a digest of a low-entropy var is a stable fingerprint); `record.data` kept unredacted though user-supplied; `Manifest.SchemaVersion` value and the `Chain` enum unspecified; the export is named `audit.jsonl` with stale `seq`/`prev` — a copy-back into `.metareview/runs/<id>/` fails closed (`ERR_AUDIT_CHAIN` at seq 2), which is fine, but naming it `audit.redacted.jsonl` prevents the attempt. — evidence: spec §5 lines 93-104; `internal/fsm/run/store.go:86-90`; spec 2 line 216.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] Copied Origin-bearing transitions never set `FixEntryHead` (fold 4.6), and step 8 rebaselines only when the node at `From` is agent-edit; a fork from a later state in the same iteration leaves `FixEntryHead == ""`. `commit_exists` fails closed (`ERR_GATE_INAPPLICABLE`) rather than silently, so no corruption, but worth a sentence and an F4 row. — evidence: spec §2 step 8 (lines 63-64); `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:202`; `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go:99-101`.

NOTES: Surfaces checked and found sound: copied-event byte identity (`Data` already canonical; `LineHash` over the newline-stripped line matches `parseLines`' `prev` computation, store.go:93), `Origin.Seq == child seq` consistent with copying P `2..seq` and `ForkedAtSeq = seq` (fold.go:414-422), `Sidecar.List` includes `workflow.yaml` and excludes `audit.*`/`lock`, child fold needs no parent (parent deletion → `parent_missing`, never fatal), mock-run forks keep `Mock` on every non-Origin event. The two blocking items are both "same id, two truths" problems (runs.jsonl verdict; prefix vs sidecar) — neither needs a schema bump to fix.

### Feasibility — attempt 2 (r3)

### Scope and alignment — attempt 2 (r3)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 70] [blocking] §6 claims "That is the same-target rule for FSM runs: three attempts on one lineage without a PASS stops retries", but the mechanism only refuses a fork whose *immediate parent's* row is ESCALATED (`Deps.Escalated(P.RunID)`, §2 step 0). Two same-target retries stay open and neither is ledgered: (a) forking any earlier ancestor of the escalated leaf (run 2's row is NEEDS_REVISION, so `--from … --run <run2>` is accepted; its child is attempt 3 again, and this can repeat forever), and (b) a fresh `fsm init` on the same `<workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>` target — no `Escalated` hook exists on `Init` (spec 2 §5.2/§5.3, spec 5 §2). The design this file borrows its vocabulary from locks the opposite: "ESCALATED remains blocking for the same scope and target", "no previous run, but the latest run for the same scope and target has verdict ESCALATED: fail fast", and the existing `runchain.escalatedForTarget` enforces exactly that (`runchain.go:155-165`, incl. its head-moved reset). The §9 row "ESCALATED = third non-PASS attempt of a lineage" records the producer, not the narrowed enforcement. Either check by scope+target (and hand `Init` the same check to spec 2/5), or ledger "leaf-only refusal; same-target init and ancestor forks are not blocked" with a rationale and drop the equivalence claim. — evidence: target §2 step 0, §6 lines "That is the same-target rule…" and "`record.Escalated(root)` … reports whether `runID`'s row is ESCALATED"; `docs/specs/2026-05-28-bounded-review-fsm-escalation.md` lines 24, 136-141, 206; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:49-92,155-165`; `CLAUDE.md` completion rule.

### Architecture — attempt 2 (r3)

VERDICT: PASS_ADVISORY

FINDINGS:
- [P2] [confidence 100] [advisory] §2 step 7 claims the in-memory `run.FoldFull` catches `MaxEvents`. It cannot: `FoldFull`/`Apply` (`internal/fsm/run/fold.go:22-248`) have no event-count cap; the cap lives only in `prepareAppend` (`internal/fsm/run/store.go:143-152`) and is driven by `Options.MaxEvents`, which the `RunStore` interface does not expose (`store.go:50-60`). So a fork of a near-cap parent passes step 7, `run.Create` succeeds, and a step-8 `Append` (copies and the child's own `tree` are `countedType`, `store.go:66-73`) fails with `ERR_AUDIT_FULL`, leaving an incomplete child — the one enumerated cause that step 7 promises to catch "before any I/O" and F5/F10 cannot test as written. Either add `MaxEvents() int` to `RunStore` (ledger it in §9 with `TornFiles`) and count `countedType` events in step 7, or delete `MaxEvents` from the step-7 list and document it as a step-8 incomplete-fork cause. — evidence: spec §2 step 7, §9 "run spec §11 (handoff)"; `fold.go:235-248`; `store.go:118-156`
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] `ERR_FORK_INCOMPLETE ≡ Seq ≤ ForkedAtSeq` misses the agent-edit crash window: a crash after the child's `tree` (seq `ForkedAtSeq+1`) but before `fix_baseline` yields a child that opens as complete, with `StateKind == agent-edit` and `FixEntryHead == ""` (copied transitions never set it, `fold.go:202-204`; only `fix_baseline` does, `fold.go:208-218`). Its first `fix → verify` evaluation then fails closed as `ERR_GATE_INAPPLICABLE` → `failed`, a confusing terminal outcome instead of the designed refusal. Tighten the predicate to `ParentRunID != "" && (Seq ≤ ForkedAtSeq || (Seq == ForkedAtSeq+1 && StateKind == KindAgentEdit))`, and add the row to F11. — evidence: spec §2 step 8, step 10 last sentence, §8 F11; `fold.go:202-218`
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] §6 asserts "That is the same-target rule for FSM runs: three attempts on one lineage without a PASS stops retries", but `Deps.Escalated(P)` checks only P's own row, and `attemptNumber = len(Lineage)+1` is depth, not attempt count. Forking any non-escalated ancestor (root or attempt-2 child) after a grandchild escalates restarts at attempt 2/3 with no limit on siblings, so retries on one target (`<workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>`) are unbounded. The review-side rule it claims to mirror is target-wide (`internal/runchain/runchain.go:155-165` `escalatedForTarget`, checked before any new attempt at `runchain.go:88-90`). Either make `record.Escalated(P)` true when any ESCALATED row's `previousRunId` chain (`runchain.ChainTo`) contains P, or drop the "same-target rule" sentence and state that FSM escalation is per-node and a human fork of an ancestor is a deliberate reset. — evidence: spec §6 verdict map paragraph, §2 step 0; `runchain.go:49-92,155-165`
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] Export's per-kind `node_output` redaction has no seam to learn a node's kind or its output shape. The event carries `Node`, not kind (`internal/fsm/run/event.go:6-18`); mapping node→kind requires parsing `workflow.yaml` with `Kinds.Info()` (`machine.go:356`), but `Export`'s declared deps are `Store, Sidecar, FS, Clock` and its imports "run, machine (types only)". The table then hard-codes spec 4 field names (`desc`, `rejected[]`) in `export`, duplicating the kinds' schema knowledge, and "other kinds: kept" is fail-open for any kind the table does not name. Give `export` a `Kinds machine.Registry` (or a `map[kind]Redactor` seam owned by `kind`, next to `kind.Decision`), map state→node→kind through the parsed sidecar, and make unnamed kinds fail closed (drop free-text fields or the whole output). Also: `Export(ctx, deps Deps /* …FS… */)` cannot be `machine.Deps` — it has no `FS` field (`internal/fsm/machine/types.go:85-100`) and §1 adds only `Escalated`; say `export.Deps`. — evidence: spec §1, §5 signature + redaction table rows 10-12; `types.go:85-100`; `machine.go:348-361`
- [P3] [confidence 75] [advisory] Step 10 "Return the child machine (`Open`ed)" will fail if step 8's child lock is still held: `Open` → `load` → `Store.Lock` refuses a run already held by this process (`internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go:314-318`, `store_mem.go:127-129`). State that the child's step-8 lock is released before step 10 (or build the child's `View` from the step-8 session without re-`Open`). — evidence: spec §2 steps 8, 10; `store_jsonl.go:307-339`; `machine.go:285-311`
- [P3] [confidence 75] [advisory] Crash between `run.Create` and `Sidecar.Write` (step 8) leaves an init-only child; `load` folds and then reads the sidecar (`machine.go:341-354`), so unless the `ERR_FORK_INCOMPLETE` check is specified to run immediately after the fold, `Open` reports `ERR_SIDECAR{missing}` instead. State the check's position in `load` (after `FoldFull`, before `Sidecar.Read`) and in `run.summarize` for `List`. — evidence: spec §2 step 8; `machine.go:341-354`; `store.go:179-195`
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] The fork's own `tree`/`fix_baseline` must carry spec 2's stamps, in particular `Mock: P.Mock != ""`: `Apply` rejects a non-`Origin`, non-`Mock` event on a mock run as `mock_stamp` (`fold.go:85-87`), so a mock parent would fail every fork at the `tree` seq with `ERR_COPY_INVALID`. Step 7 lists `Mock` for `init` and copies only; add "stamped per spec 2 §5.4 tail (`At: Clock()`, `State`/`Iter` of the checkpoint, `Mock`)". — evidence: spec §2 step 7; `fold.go:85-87`; `machine.go:409-415`
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] `detail_summary {files, insertions, deletions}` for `commit_exists` is a structured value derived by parsing free text: `GateError.Detail` is `count + porcelain + WorkingDiff` already cut by `CapDetail` (`machine.go:474-477`), so the counts are computed from possibly truncated text and a heuristic parser export must own.
