NOTES: Package placement is sound: `Deps.Escalated`/`Deps.Terminal` are func seams, so `machine` never imports `record` (which imports `runchain`+`state`) and no cycle forms; `record` rows decode through `runchain.Record` (`runchain.go:32-47`) and the only other reader is `internal/reviewlog/reviewlog.go:53` via `ReadRuns`, so the additive keys are safe. Lock ordering P→child is acyclic and non-blocking (`LOCK_NB`), so no deadlock; the P lock spanning the child's `Create`/fsyncs is acceptable. `RunStore.TornFiles` is coherent with `ValidSidecarName` refusing `audit.*` (`sidecar.go:22-24`), so torn bytes are reachable only through the store. The Diff alignment key survives a judge swap because spec 4's `InputHash` preimage (`judge-kinds.md` §3.1: `{golden, candidate}` / `{diff, …, candidate}` / `{bug, diff, …}`) excludes model and effort — good. Record idempotency is fine: `Terminal` fires on every advance of a terminal run (`machine.go:540-547`) and the first-row-wins rule makes `updatedAt` effectively write-once; say which field feeds `headSha` (presumably `Snapshot.Head`). The compatibility invariant (name + kind for copied states and `From`) is sufficient for the fold's `to_kind`/`fix_baseline_kind` checks and for `NodeFor`-keyed `NodeOutputs`; `RepoMode` divergence is neutralised by `load` overriding `parsed.RepoMode = snap.RepoMode` (`machine.go:360`). Freeze rule dependency holds: `Resolve` copies the workflow struct (`resolve.go:56`), so `Refs`/`CmdRefs` survive, and `VarsReferencedBy` already unions node and cmd refs (`workflow.go:744-750`). `record`/`Escalated` reading `runs.jsonl` via `ReadRuns` fails closed on any malformed line anywhere in the file (`runchain.go:111-114`), which will also refuse forks — acceptable, worth a sentence.

### Intent preservation — attempt 2 (r3)

VERDICT: PASS_ADVISORY

FINDINGS:
- [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] ESCALATED is a *lineage* rule presented as the "same-target rule", and it collides with §6's own recovery guidance. `Deps.Escalated(P.RunID)` only refuses forking the ESCALATED run itself (§2 step 0, L47); forking its non-escalated parent (attempt 2) or a fresh `fsm init` on the identical `<workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>` target restarts retries unhindered, whereas the existing review gates enforce per target (`escalatedForTarget`, `internal/runchain/runchain.go:155-165`) and `CLAUDE.md` says ESCALATED "stops same-target retries". Meanwhile §6 L194-195 states that for `overflow` "raising the budget or swapping the judge in a fork is its prescribed recovery", yet at attempt 3 that exact fork is refused with `ERR_RUN_ESCALATED`. Depth-counting also treats §17 judge-validation forks as retries: on `review-loop` a gpt→opus→third-judge lineage that legitimately finds bugs each time (`reviewed` ×3) is ESCALATED and cannot try a fourth judge. Either say plainly it is a lineage-depth rule (L197 "That is the same-target rule for FSM runs" overclaims) and document the ancestor-fork bypass as intended, or exempt var/workflow-changing forks from the attempt count. — evidence: target §6 L194-201, §2 step 0 L47, §9 L233-234; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:155-165`; `CLAUDE.md` Completion Rule; design §17, §10.1.
- [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] "Fork, then commit" flips the design's recovery order ("fix the commit, resume from verify", design §10.1 L258-262; plan §1.7 step 6 "commit, `advance`, `record`, `advance`"). The recovery is delivered, but the reflexive commit-first path now fails `HEAD == ch` with a hint (`git worktree add <dir> <ch>`) that produces a tree *without* the operator's fix commit and says nothing about carrying it over (cherry-pick into the worktree, or `git reset --soft <ch>` then fork then re-commit). The `Detail` hint and spec 5's `GATE_FAILED` `resume_hint` (spec 5 §3, which today just says `advance --run <id> --from <state>`) should state the order and the commit-first recovery. — evidence: target §2 step 6 L77-81, F2 L218, §9 L235; design §10.1; plan §1.7 step 6; spec 5 §3.
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] `Diff` rows and the export have no *subject*: `llm_call` carries only `Kind/Model/Effort/Index/InputHash/Verdict` (spec 4 §3.6; run spec §3.1), so a `CallRow` keyed by `(node, iter, kind, input_hash)` tells the §17 reader that *a* decision flipped but not which finding or golden it concerns; recovering that requires recomputing `Canonical(input)` with the exact cut diff. The design's "diffed cell-by-cell" (§10) implies finding × judge cells. Handoff to spec 4 (schema is still v1 pre-release): add a subject (`BugID(IssueText)` / golden index) to `LLMCallData`, or have `Diff` surface a `Subject` by joining `node_output` confirmed/rejected ids. — evidence: target §4 L118-128; spec 4 §3.6, §4.2; run spec §3.1 `llm_call`; design §10, §14.3, §17.
- [P2] [confidence 65] [advisory] `Decision` is the raw bool, not the kind's *effective* decision. Adjudicate's rule is `is_real && confidence ≥ 0.7` (spec 4 §3.1; `internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:463`), so `true/0.9` vs `true/0.6` reports `DecisionSame: true` while one candidate was confirmed and the other rejected — precisely the flip §17 wants to see; `Same` (exact confidence equality) is almost never true across judges, so the discriminating signal is the wrong one. Add an `Effective *bool` (or make `DecisionFunc` apply the kind rule) and/or diff the `confirmed`/`rejected` sets from `node_output`. Also `kind.Decision` is referenced as existing (L115, §9 L239/L245) but spec 4 r5 declares no such accessor (its §9 only says "spec 3 Diff compares decision fields + confidence"), and no `Decision(kind, verdict)` exists in `internal/fsm/kind`. — evidence: target §4 L115, L123-126, §9 L239, L245; spec 4 §3.1 rule column, §9; `kind.go:442,463`.
- [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] Goldens redaction is self-inconsistent: `init.goldens[].comment` is emptied (L151) but every matched bug carries `Desc = Golden.Comment` (spec 4 §4.2 step 1) in `confirmed`, which is kept (L158), and again in `delta_applied` (L159). Either golden text is sensitive (then the export still leaks it) or it is not (then the §17 reader loses the golden list for nothing). Pick one. — evidence: target §5 L151, L158-159; spec 4 §4.2 step 1.
- [P3] [confidence 60] [follow-up] Export auditability nits: the review-lenses row says findings keep `id`, but `run.Finding` has no `id` field (`internal/fsm/run/types.go:62-69`); the reader's only path from `rejected[].id` back to the hallucinated text is recomputing `BugID(issue_text)` — say so, since `rejected[].desc` is emptied. `converge.reason` is emptied wholesale (L154), which removes the built-in atoms' harmless stop reason (iteration/budget numbers) that a `STOPPED` reader needs; redact only `cmd`-atom reasons. — evidence: target §5 L154, L157-158; spec 2 §5.4 step 6; `types.go:62-69`.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [follow-up] `mock` row = `Snapshot.Mock != ""` ignores `MockTainted`; a tainted hand-built log would record `mock: false`. Use `Mock != "" || MockTainted`. — evidence: target §6 L188; run spec §4.8 (`MockTainted`).

NOTES: Attempt-1 intent findings are materially resolved — `snapshot.json` is a redacted projection (§5 L165-166), the `forked` row is gone (§6, §9 L232), `Diff` separates `DecisionSame`/`ConfidenceSame` and aligns by input hash (§4), review-lenses findings keep `severity/category/source`, and the ESCALATED mechanism is now explicit (`Deps.Escalated`, no `runchain.Resolve`). The judge-swap recipe is stated honestly (sdlc-loop only at `--at-iter 0`, L103-104; review-loop has no loop so "any time" holds). Wording rule: "deterministic" appears only for `Diff` report ordering (L127-128, F7 "determinism (two calls equal)") — no "deterministic results" claim anywhere in r3. What the §17 reader still sees in an export: model/effort/kind/input_hash/verdict incl. `reasoning`/confidence/tokens per call, `confirmed` with desc, `rejected` ids only, findings' `issue_text`, transitions/gates (detail emptied except `commit_exists` summary); what they cannot see: the diff bytes, `converge.reason`, `gate.error.detail`, cmd streams, and (per the P2 above) which finding each `llm_call` adjudicated. No lost or inverted design intent found; the P2s are precision and usefulness gaps, not reversals.

### Security — attempt 2 (r3)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P2] [confidence 80] [blocking] Golden-comment redaction is self-defeating: the table empties `init.goldens[].comment` (spec §5 row 4), but the identical text is persisted as `Bug.Desc` for every matched golden and the same bundle keeps it in three places — `node_output.output.confirmed` for `match-then-adjudicate` (row: "`confirmed` kept"), `delta_applied.confirmed[].desc` (row only empties `findings[].desc`), and `snapshot.json` (the mirror list at §5 "`Vars`, `AllowedCmds`, `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, `Mock`, `TreeStatus`, `LastError.Detail`, `NodeOutputs`" omits `Snapshot.Goldens`, `Confirmed`, `AllFound`). Whatever the redaction protects (the calibration answer key) is fully recoverable from the export. Either drop the goldens row (declare comments not sensitive) or extend it to `Snapshot.Goldens[].comment` and to `desc` of bugs with `verdict == matched` in `node_output`/`delta_applied`/snapshot `Confirmed`/`AllFound`, and add an F8 marker row for it. — evidence: `internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:449-450` (`desc, _ := run.CapText(golden.Comment, run.MaxDesc)` … `run.Bug{ID: run.BugID(golden.Comment), Desc: desc, …Verdict: run.VerdictMatched`), `internal/fsm/run/types.go:216-219` (`Goldens`, `Confirmed`, `AllFound` in `Snapshot`), spec §5 rows 4, 11, 12 and the snapshot sentence.
- [P2] [confidence 85] [advisory] `Deps.Escalated` trusts an unchained, locally editable file when the chained source already carries the answer: `record.Escalated(root)` reads `.metareview/runs.jsonl` (§6), so deleting or editing the ESCALATED row (or any crash/`Terminal` error path where the transition is durable but the row was never written — spec 2 M9 "`Terminal` error returned with the transition durable") makes `Fork` step 0 pass. `attemptNumber = len(Lineage)+1` and `Outcome` are both in P's hash-chained `audit.jsonl`; compute escalation from P's snapshot (`len(P.Lineage)+1 ≥ maxAttempts && verdict(P.Outcome) != PASS`) and treat `runs.jsonl` as the cross-tool record only (or check both, refusing on either). — evidence: spec §2 step 0, §6 last paragraph; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:193-194` (`Lineage`, `ForkedAtSeq` in `Snapshot`); CLAUDE.md "Keep transient `.metareview/runs.jsonl` … local".
- [P2] [confidence 80] [advisory] The "same-target rule" is per-chain, not per-target: after R→C1→C2 (C2 ESCALATED at attempt 3), forking R again yields `Lineage=[R]`, attempt 2, and the loop restarts indefinitely, though all runs share `target.id = <workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>`. The existing review gates escalate per target via `runchain`; this spec claims equivalence ("That is the same-target rule for FSM runs") but only enforces per lineage. Either count non-PASS rows per `target.id` in `Escalated`, or say plainly that ancestors stay forkable. — evidence: spec §6 "Verdict map" and "`attemptNumber = len(Lineage) + 1`"; §2 step 0 checks only `P.RunID`.
- [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] `--include-vars` writes cleartext var values into the default, committed destination: `Out` defaults to `<repo root>/docs/metareview/fsm/<run-id>/`, CLAUDE.md instructs committing `docs/metareview/`, and the only guardrail is "must never be committed (spec 5 docs)". Make it mechanical: with `IncludeVars` refuse the default `Out` (require an explicit path) and/or drop a `.gitignore` (`*`) plus a `manifest.include_vars: true` into the bundle. — evidence: spec §5 "Destination safety" first sentence and last sentence; `CLAUDE.md` "Durable Output".
- [P3] [confidence 70] [advisory] `init.vars` hashing is a confirmation oracle and weaker than it reads: unsalted `sha256:<hex>` of short values (model names, efforts, flags) is dictionary-recoverable, and any var that reaches `llm_call.model`/`effort` (row "other fields kept"), node params, or a workflow **default** (`workflow.yaml` verbatim) is in cleartext anyway. Under the spec-4 "vars are not secrets" stance this is acceptable, but the spec should say which vars `IncludeVars=false` actually hides (CLI-supplied values referenced only from cmd argv) or salt with the run id. — evidence: spec §5 rows 1, 14, 17; `internal/fsm/workflow/resolve.go:61,71` (vars substituted into `Model`/`Effort`/`Params`); spec 4 line 295.
- [P3] [confidence 70] [advisory] Substring replacement (`len ≥ 4`, `$NAME`) is underspecified at its edges: (a) values of 1–3 bytes are exported verbatim — state it; (b) replacement order is unspecified — a value that is a substring of another value, of `$OTHER`, or of a path segment (e.g. `REVIEWER=claude` → `/usr/local/bin/$REVIEWER`) gives order-dependent output; require longest-value-first single pass; (c) two `file_hashes` keys can collapse to one key after substitution — define the collision behaviour (F8 covers a marker inside a key but not a collision). — evidence: spec §5 row 2; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:151` (`FileHashes map[string]string`).
- [P3] [confidence 65] [advisory] "then paths relativised (below)" applies the `repo_root` rule (`<outside>` when outside the repo) to `argv`/`file_hashes` keys, which would replace `/usr/bin/python3` with `<outside>` and destroy the interpreter evidence; the alternative (keep absolute) leaks `$HOME`-derived usernames (`/Users/<name>/…`). Choose (suggest: inside-repo → relative, else keep verbatim except a `$HOME` prefix → `~`) and state it. — evidence: spec §5 rows 2–3; `internal/fsm/workflow/resolve.go:110-122` (argv[0] pinned absolute via `lookPath`).
- [P3] [confidence 65] [advisory] Destination-safety wording: "every path component under `Out`" must mean every component from the filesystem root (or repo root) down to and including `Out`, not only components created by `MkdirAll`; also the `Lstat`→`Create` window on intermediate directories is a TOCTOU that `O_NOFOLLOW` (final component only) does not close — acceptable for a local single-user tool, but say so. - [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] `record.data` verbatim: `RecordData.Name` is only `shortOK` and `Data` is any JSON ≤ `MaxPayload`; an agent driver that pipes tool output into `fsm record <event> --data` puts it in the export unredacted. - [P3] [confidence 70] [follow-up] Incomplete-fork detection (`Seq ≤ ForkedAtSeq`) misses two crash windows: after `tree` but before `fix_baseline` (agent-edit `From`) the child opens "complete" and `commit_exists` fails closed as `ERR_GATE_INAPPLICABLE` (safe but misleading); between `run.Create` and `Sidecar.Write` the child surfaces `ERR_SIDECAR{missing}` rather than `ERR_FORK_INCOMPLETE`. NOTES: Verified sound against the implementation: `AllowCustomCmds` re-consent covers argv, pinned file hashes and env names because all are in `AllowedCmd` → `CmdsSHA256` (`workflow/resolve.go:130,139-144`; `machine.go:96-102`), so a workflow change that renames/re-points a cmd cannot execute without a fresh sha; `WorkDir` CommonDir check mirrors `Init` (`machine.go:104-116`) and `RealExec` scrubs `GIT_*` (`gate/git.go:79-88`); store/sidecar paths are `ValidateRunID`-gated with `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` 0600 and symlink-refusing component walks (`store_jsonl.go:53-74,125,207`; `machine/sidecar.go:64,87`); building/folding the child in memory before `run.Create` and checking `MaxBytes` before the first write are the right shapes; `VerifyOrigin`'s content check plus `Origin.Hash = LineHash` (chained through `prev`) is adequate and correctly non-fatal; mock inheritance is re-verified by `MockLoad` on `Open`. Minor: the compatibility invariant checks node name+kind but not `exec` for copied states — the in-memory fold catches decode mismatches, so this is only a clarity gap. Nothing in the diff reaches a shell, a network fetch, or HTML output; the OWASP surface here is A02 (secret carriers in exports), A04 (guardrail placement), and A08 (integrity of the escalation record).

### Testing-quality — attempt 2 (r3)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P2] [confidence 75] [blocking] The `ch` rule (§2 step 6: `ch` = the checkpoint event's `TransitionData.Head`, or `InitData.Head` for `seq = 1`) has no discriminating fixture. Same for the restart case (`--at-iter 0` on initial: `expected == InitData.Head`, not latest). — evidence: spec §2 step 6 (l.77-81), §8 F2/F4 (l.218, l.220); `internal/fsm/machine/harness_test.go:315`; `internal/fsm/gate/fake.go:13-16`

F1's two-iteration rows cover `--from discover --at-iter 0` (→ seq 1) and `--from adjudicate` nil/`--at-iter 2`, but never `--from <non-initial> --at-iter 0`. A plausible wrong implementation that special-cases `*AtIter == 0` before searching transitions returns `seq = 1` (a silent restart discarding iter-0 discover output) and stays green: F3's "sdlc-loop at iter 0 ok" for `JUDGE` is also satisfied by a restart (nothing ran → nothing frozen). Add: `--from adjudicate --at-iter 0` → `ForkedAtSeq` equals the iter-0 `adjudicate` transition seq, `Copied > 0`, discover executor call count 0 on the child's first advance. — evidence: spec §2 step 1 (l.48-53), §8 F1 (l.217), F3 (l.219)
- [P2] [confidence 90] [blocking] F8's "`O_EXCL` creation (fake `FS` records flags)" cannot be true with the declared seam: `FS.Create(path string) (io.WriteCloser, error)` carries no flags or mode, so the fake has nothing to record and the `O_CREATE|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY|O_NOFOLLOW` 0600 rule lives only in a comment on the interface. - [P2] [confidence 65] [blocking] The freeze rule for `overflow_handler` (§2 step 3: `K ∈ w.CmdRefs[c]` for any `cmd` named by a copied `cmd_call`/`overflow_handler`; `state` = P's state at the time) has no row. ### Data-migration — attempt 2 (r3)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P2] [confidence 75] [blocking] `ERR_FORK_INCOMPLETE` is defined as `Seq ≤ ForkedAtSeq` ("no rebaseline `tree`"), but step 8 writes `tree` then, for an agent-edit `From`, `fix_baseline`. A crash between those two leaves a child with `Seq == ForkedAtSeq+1` that the rule classifies as complete: `Open` succeeds, `FixEntryHead` stays `""` (copied transitions never set it — `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:214-216`), `commit_exists` fails closed → the child terminates `failed`, gets a `NEEDS_REVISION` row, and counts as an attempt toward ESCALATED (§6). Nobody can repair it either: `fix_baseline` must immediately follow its `tree` (`fold.go:222-231`). A crash artifact is silently reinterpreted as a legitimate failed attempt. Define incompleteness as `Seq ≤ ForkedAtSeq`, or agent-edit `From` (from the checkpoint's `ToKind`/`InitialKind`) with no `fix_baseline` at `ForkedAtSeq+2`; and state which check wins when the sidecar is also missing (crash between `run.Create` and `Sidecar.Write`, step 8 order) so spec 5's manual-deletion doc and F11 match — evidence: spec §2 step 7–8 (lines 79–89), §7, F11; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:214-216,222-231`; `internal/fsm/run/store.go:180-197` (`summarize` has no such rule today).

Cross-spec drift to flag to the orchestrator (not my lens): spec 5 line 112 still wires `Terminal = machine.RecordTerminal(root)` while spec 3 moved it to `record.Terminal(root, clock)`; and `record.Escalated` is by row id only, so forking a sibling of an ESCALATED grandchild restarts the count at `attemptNumber 2` — the "same-target rule" in §6 is lineage-depth, not per-target like `escalatedForTarget`. F9's "a missing key fails" is not what `DisallowUnknownFields` does (it only rejects extra keys); a required-key assertion needs its own check.

### Feasibility — attempt 3 (r4)

**VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION**

(Reviewed the file as it stands on disk — it moved to r5 mid-review. r5 already fixes what I had flagged against r4: `Snapshot.WorkflowSource` for the recorder, the nonexistent `MatchThreshold` (`kind.go:434-442` is a relative argmax with `best = 0.0`; `AdjudicateThreshold` at `kind.go:46` is the only per-call bar), `Decision` moving to `machine` because `kind` imports `machine`, persisted verdicts carrying `confidence` (`judge/prompts.go:281-295`), and `rejected[].desc`. **FINDINGS:**

- [P2] [confidence 90] [blocking] `CommonPrefixSeq` as defined in §4 cannot equal `ForkedAtSeq` for any fork of a root parent, contradicting F7 and the spec's own parenthetical. The tuple is `(Type, Node, Data, Origin != nil)` compared across both logs; a root parent's events `2..seq` have `Origin == nil` while the child's copies carry `Origin` (§2 step 7; enforced by `checkProvenance`, `run/fold.go:413-425`), so the tuple differs at seq 2 and the result is always `1`. For a fork-of-fork it breaks at `P.ForkedAtSeq+1`. The definition and the test row are mutually exclusive, so an implementer must guess. Minimal fix: compare `(Type, Node, At, Data)` ignoring `Origin` (copies keep `At` verbatim, the child's own `tree` gets a fresh `Clock()` `At` — `machine.go:409-416`), or define `CommonPrefixSeq := b.ForkedAtSeq` when `b.ParentRunID == a.RunID` and fall back to the scan otherwise. — evidence: spec §4 lines 91-93, F7 row; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:413-425`; `internal/fsm/machine/machine.go:409-416`
- [P3] [confidence 85] [advisory] F5's "renamed node under the same state key" cannot be constructed: `Node.Name` is always the state key (`workflow.go:350`, `node := &Node{Name: name,…}` with `name` ranging over the `nodes` map keys), so the "same node **name**" clause in step 2's invariant is vacuous. Drop the clause and the row, or say "state key". — evidence: spec §2 step 2, F5; `internal/fsm/workflow/workflow.go:338-352`
- [P3] [confidence 85] [advisory] F8 copy-back names the wrong layer: `Store.Events` only decodes the envelope and verifies the chain (`run/store.go:76-98`); payload decoding with `DisallowUnknownFields` happens in `Apply` (`fold.go:429-470`). The fail-closed guarantee holds; the row should assert on `Open`/`FoldFull`. `ERR_AUDIT_CHAIN` at seq 2 for an advanced run is correct. — evidence: spec §5 last paragraph, F8; `internal/fsm/run/store.go:76-98`
- [P3] [confidence 80] [advisory] F1 "copied prefix equals parent `[2..seq]` byte-for-byte modulo `Origin`" is unsatisfiable literally: `prev` also differs on every copied line (the child chains from its own `init`; `prepareAppend` re-stamps `Seq`/`Prev`, `store.go:118-131`). ### Completeness — attempt 3 (r4)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:

### Architecture — attempt 3 (r4)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 100] [blocking] **Import cycle in the stated package placement.** §1 (line 30) puts `Diff` in `internal/fsm/machine/diff.go`, and §4 (line 140) gives it the signature `Diff(a, b run.Log, decide func(kind string, verdict json.RawMessage) kind.Decision)` — the result type `kind.Decision` is declared in `internal/fsm/kind/decision.go` (§1 lines 33, 49). But `kind` already imports `machine` (`internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:20`, for `ExecInput`/`NodeKind`/`Registry`), and `machine` today imports nothing from `kind` (verified). As written, `machine → kind → machine` cannot compile, so the `decide` injection — clearly intended to keep `machine` free of `kind` — is defeated by its own return type. Remedy is a type relocation, not a redesign: declare `Decision{Raw, Effective *bool}` in `machine` (or `run`) and have `kind.Decision(...)` return that type, or have `decide` return `(raw, effective *bool)`. Update §1 line 49, §4 line 140, and the spec-4 ledger mirror (§9 line 302) accordingly. — evidence: `docs/specs/2026-08-27-metareview-0.9.0-fsm-fork.md:30,33,49,140`; `internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:17-22`.
- [P2] [confidence 90] [advisory] **"Tightened kind `Decode`" cannot be a `*FoldError`.** §2 step 7 (lines 111-113) and F5 (line 274) attribute the Decode re-validation of copied `node_output`s to `run.FoldFull`, but `run` has no kinds: `Apply` on `NodeOutputData` only canonicalizes and stores the raw output (`internal/fsm/run/fold.go:103-111`); it can produce `output_hash` (`fold.go:120`), unsanctioned-cmd (`fold.go:166-170`), mock-stamp (`fold.go:86`) and provenance errors, never a Decode error. The spec should state an explicit machine-side pass in step 7 (for each copied `node_output`, `Kinds.Kind(w.NodeFor(State).Kind).Decode(output)` → `ERR_COPY_INVALID{seq, reason: decode}`), still before any I/O. Feasible; just misattributed. — evidence: spec lines 111-114, 274; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:103-111`.
- [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] **`CommonPrefixSeq` definition vs F7 row.** §4 defines the prefix over identical `(Type, Node, Data)` (line 152-153), yet F7 asserts `CommonPrefixSeq == ForkedAtSeq` for a non-restart fork and that a child `tree` "type-equal-data to the parent's next event does not extend it" (line 276). If P's seq+1 is itself a `tree` at the same head with the same tree hash/status (a plausible rebaseline at an unchanged tree), the definition *does* extend the prefix to `ForkedAtSeq+1`. Either include `Origin` presence in the tuple (copied events carry it; the child's own `tree` does not) or clarify F7 to "type-equal, data-different". — evidence: spec lines 152-153, 276; `internal/fsm/run/event.go:16`.
- [P3] [confidence 75] [advisory] **Tolerant scanner is `record`-local; `runchain.ReadRuns` is not tolerant.** §6 (lines 221-223) gives `record` its own scanner that skips a torn final line, but the same file is read by `runchain.ReadRuns`, which fails on any undecodable line (`internal/runchain/runchain.go:105-113`). A crash mid-append of an FSM row (`state.AppendJSONL` is a single unlocked `O_APPEND` write, `internal/state/state.go:17-31`) therefore leaves the review gates (`taskdone`/`prready` via `runchain.Resolve`) unable to read `runs.jsonl` until a human truncates it — a new failure mode this spec introduces into a shared file. Pre-existing fragility, but the spec adds a writer that runs on every FSM terminal; consider a `record`-side self-heal (truncate a torn tail before appending, as `RepairTail` does for audit.jsonl) or a ledger row handing tolerance to `runchain`. — evidence: spec lines 221-225; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:105-113`; `internal/state/state.go:17-31`.

NOTES:
Verified as sound against the implemented packages (no finding): (1) lock ordering — `Lock` requires `audit.jsonl` to exist (`store_jsonl.go:311`) so child `Lock` must follow `Create` exactly as step 8 orders; `Append` requires the in-process `held` entry (`store_jsonl.go:196`); per-run `flock` files make holding P + child simultaneously safe; `Open` unlocks after `load` (`machine.go:285-294`) so step 10's "P released, child re-taken by `load`" matches. (2) Post-terminal `fork` on P is permitted by the fold (`fold.go:333-339` allows `TypeFork`, `TypeTree`), so "a terminal P is allowed" works. (3) Incomplete-fork predicate is consistent with the fold: copied transitions with `Origin` never set `FixEntryHead` (`fold.go:202`), root-only init baseline (`fold.go:301`), `fix_baseline` requires the immediately preceding `tree` at the same head (`fold.go:208-218`); `Snapshot.StateKind`/`ForkedAtSeq`/`Lineage` exist (`types.go:194-210`); placing the check after `FoldFull` and before `Sidecar.Read` in `load` (`machine.go:341-348`) is a two-line insertion. (4) Count fidelity — `prepareAppend` rejects when `n+1 > maxEvents` over counted existing lines (`store.go:143-152`), which is exactly `count(Counted) ≤ MaxEvents`; `tree` is counted (`store.go:69`). Implementation note: the in-memory `FoldFull` needs provisional `Seq` values on the `tree`/`fix_baseline` events (`fold.go:33`), which `Append` re-assigns identically. (5) Escalation from the chained snapshot — §2 step 0 and §6's verdict map agree (`attempt = len(Lineage)+1`, `≥3 ∧ terminal ∧ non-PASS`); the machine calls `Terminal` on `finish`, `fail`, and on every `Advance` of a terminal run (`machine.go:540-548, 783-790, 831-835`), so "transition durable, resume retries" is true and idempotency-by-id is what makes the retry safe. (6) `record`/`export` seams: `Deps.Terminal func(ctx, View) error` exists (`types.go:99`); `export` imports `machine` only for `Sidecar`/`Registry`/`Clock` and `record` only for `View` — neither is imported by `machine`, so no cycle there. (7) `Pw.VarsReferencedBy`/`CmdRefs` exist on the resolved workflow (`workflow.go:106-107, 743-747`); `LLMCallData` carries `Kind`, `Index`, `InputHash`, `Confidence` (`event.go:95-106`) so Diff's alignment key and `CallSide` are populated from stored data. (8) Compatibility invariant: `ReasonUnsanctionedCmd` in the fold catches a copied `cmd_call` against the child's new `AllowedCmds`, so the `cmd` clause is enforced twice (declared-name check + fold). The only blocker is the `machine`↔`kind` cycle; every other item is advisory or a wording fix.

### Intent preservation — attempt 3 (r4)

VERDICT: PASS_ADVISORY

FINDINGS:
- [P2] [confidence 60] [advisory] The lineage-depth escalation rule is stated honestly and ledgered (§6 lines 241-251, §9 row 4), but its "human reset" clause is an assertion with no mechanism behind it. After the attempt-3 leaf is refused (`ERR_RUN_ESCALATED`), an agent can fork the non-escalated attempt-2 parent with identical vars and workflow, get a fresh attempt 3, and repeat indefinitely — F5 (line 274) explicitly asserts "its non-escalated parent still forkable". Nothing distinguishes that from a human reset. The rationale (line 246-249) justifies sibling re-runs with different vars/workflows for §17 experiments — but siblings forked from one parent never exceed depth 2 and were never blocked by the depth rule, so the rationale does not actually reason about the same-vars ancestor re-fork it permits. This is not an inverted intent (plan r3 §1.9, human-locked, had `maxAttempts: 0`, i.e. no cap at all; r4 is stricter), so advisory — but the bounded-review spec's "do not let agents ignore blockers after the retry cap" (2026-05-28 spec line 19) is only honoured if the spec 5 handoff adds an agent-prompt sentence in the same family as the consent rules (`ERR_RUN_ESCALATED` is for a human: do not fork an ancestor or re-`init` on your own). Spec 5 r3 §4 (lines 160-177) has consent rules for `--allow-custom-cmds`/`--accept-workflow-change` but none for escalation. — evidence: fork.md §2 step 0 (63-65), §6 (241-251), §8 F5 (274), §9 row 4 (288); plan §1.9 (321-327); escalation spec lines 19-24; cli.md §4 (160-177), §6 (243-244)
- [P3] [confidence 75] [advisory] The `rejected[].desc` redaction rule's stated reason is false: "a hallucination's text stays only in the source log" (line 193), yet the `review-lenses` `node_output.output` is "kept whole" with `issue_text` = the finding (line 192), and a rejected candidate's `Desc` is exactly one of those `issue_text`s. The redaction therefore hides nothing while forcing the §17 reader to do the `BugID(issue_text)` join to see what the judge rejected — the false-negative audit is the most important cell for the gpt-5.2 concern. Either keep `desc` (reader value, nothing lost) or state the real reason. — evidence: fork.md §5 table rows 192-193; §9 row "run spec §12 row 3"; design §14.3, §17
- [P3] [confidence 50] [follow-up] Diff usefulness vs design §16's headline swap ("the most compelling swap — your own adjudicator — is a shell command"): `Diff` compares `llm_call` rows only (§4, lines 140-156); a `cmd`-kind adjudicator emits `cmd_call` + `node_output`, so its verdicts never appear in `Calls` — only `Outcomes`/`Transitions` differ. Moreover §2 step 2's compatibility invariant requires `From` itself to keep the same kind and exec (line 79-80), so swapping a built-in adjudicate for a `cmd` adjudicator is not a fork at all but a fresh `init`, with `CommonPrefixSeq` = 1. Plan §3.5 also had only `llm_calls`, so this is consistent with the accepted plan, not a loss — but a `NodeOutputs`-level row (confirmed-set symmetric difference by `BugID`) would close the §16 story and belongs with the `Subject` follow-up to spec 4. — evidence: fork.md §2 (79-82), §4 (140-156); design §16 "Why include it"; plan §3.5 (400-405)

The "fork first, then commit" inversion of design §10.1's operator order is ledgered (§9 row 6) and is forced by the run spec's `fix_baseline_head` invariant (`payload.Head == Snapshot.Head`, run-persistence §4.6 line 223-225), not an unexplained drift; spec 5's `resume_hint` carries the same recipe. Export gives the §17 reader `llm_call.verdict` incl. `reasoning`, `confirmed`, `Findings`, `Goldens`, pairwise-verifiable unredacted lines and `ChainHead` — auditable. runs.jsonl `status` vocabulary matches the existing writers (`internal/taskdone/review.go:450-458`). ### Security — attempt 3 (r4)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 80] [blocking] The declared torn-tail tolerance of `runs.jsonl` (§6, spec lines 221-223: "skips an unterminated final line (a torn `AppendJSONL`) … a `Terminal` error leaves the transition durable and the resume path retries") is defeated by the spec's own write path. `record` imports `internal/state` (§1 line 31) and the writer it follows (`internal/taskdone/review.go:193`) is `state.AppendJSONL`, which opens with `O_APPEND` and writes `json+"\n"` without checking that the file already ends in `\n` (`/Users/dsifry/Developer/metareview/internal/state/state.go:21-31`). So the first `Terminal` after a torn tail (crash, or a crafted unterminated last line — valid or invalid JSON) glues its row onto the fragment, producing a *terminated* undecodable middle line. Consequences: (a) the appended FSM row is hidden — never decodable, never found by the by-`id` idempotency lookup; (b) every later `Terminal`/`Exists` read hits `ERR_RUNS_JSONL{line}`, so "the resume path retries" retries forever and the run can never record; (c) `runchain.ReadRuns` (`/Users/dsifry/Developer/metareview/internal/runchain/runchain.go:93-121`, called by `Resolve` for every review gate) fails on the same line, so one FSM terminal after a torn tail breaks all review gates in the repo until a human hand-edits the file. F9 only tests the read side ("torn final line skipped"). Fix in-spec: `record.Terminal` must inspect the tail before appending (prepend `\n` when the last byte is not `\n`, or refuse with `ERR_RUNS_JSONL` plus a repair hint so the row is never buried), and F9 needs a "torn tail, then `Terminal` → two decodable rows, no `ERR_RUNS_JSONL`, `runchain.ReadRuns` succeeds" row. — evidence: spec §1 L31, §6 L221-223, §8 F9 L278; `internal/state/state.go:21-31`; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:93-121`; `internal/taskdone/review.go:193`
- [P3] [confidence 70] [advisory] Redaction table accuracy vs. its "enumerates every event/field" claim (r4 header L15, L179): (1) `node_output.output — cmd` is described as `{exit, …}` with "no free text beyond capped cmd output", but the `cmd` kind's output is a `run.Delta` (`findings[].issue_text`, `confirmed[].desc`, `status`, `commit`) — free text of the same class as `review-lenses`, so "kept" is policy-consistent but the stated shape/justification is wrong (`/Users/dsifry/Developer/metareview/internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:606-647`, spec 4 §4.4); (2) `cmd_call.error` / `overflow_handler.error` are not named in the table — they are safe (bare codes via `errs.Code`, `internal/fsm/cmdexec/cmdexec.go:148-157`) but should be listed as kept; (3) the `llm_call.error` "class prefix before the first `: `" rule is dead code — `LLMCallData.Error` is already a bare code (`internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:376-378`). — evidence: spec L15, L179, L191, L194; files cited
- [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] Idempotency "by `id`" (§6 L220) with no content check means a pre-planted `runs.jsonl` row carrying the run's id (`run.RunID(name, clock)` is guessable) silently suppresses the real terminal row and never errors. Local-trust, and escalation is correctly enforced from the chained snapshot, so not exploitable against a guardrail — but the spec should either state this as accepted or make a pre-existing row whose `headSha`/`workflowHash` differ an `ERR_RUNS_JSONL`-class error. — evidence: spec L219-221, L243-245
- [P3] [confidence 55] [follow-up] `ERR_RUN_ESCALATED` requires "P terminal" (§2 step 0, L64). A non-terminal attempt-3 P (abandoned or stuck at `needs_input`) is therefore forkable without bound into attempt-4+ children; each non-PASS leaf records `escalated` and is itself refused, which is consistent with the ledgered lineage-depth rule, but §6's "human reset" sentence (L245-246) should name this case so the limit is not over-read. — evidence: spec L63-65, L241-246

NOTES: Checked and found sound: sidecar surface (`List` only regular files matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`, excludes `audit.*`/`lock`; `Read`/`Write` use `O_NOFOLLOW`, `internal/fsm/machine/sidecar.go:19-24,64,87,116`) so exported sidecars cannot traverse or follow symlinks; store `openNoFollow`/`checkComponents` (`internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go:54-62,92`); export default `Out` is safe because `<run-id>` passes `ValidateRunID`; `commit_exists` detail really does carry the working diff (`internal/fsm/gate/gates.go:123`), so the `detail_summary` replacement and the snapshot `LastError.Detail` emptying are the right calls; `Snapshot.Warnings` are codes only (`internal/fsm/run/fold.go:189`), so the omission from the snapshot mirror list is harmless; adjudicate `Reduce` drops `Rejected` (`internal/fsm/kind/kind.go:353-358`), so `rejected[].desc` exists only in `node_output` and the emptying is complete (no leak via `delta_applied`/`AllFound`); cmd env is names-only with a reserved-name denylist (`internal/fsm/workflow/workflow.go:117,298-314`, `internal/fsm/cmdexec/cmdexec.go:93-111`) so no env values reach any event; `VerifyOrigin`'s content check covers every copied field (`Data, At, State, Iter, Node, Mock`) plus version and line hash; the incomplete-fork predicate is correct for restart forks (`Seq 2 > ForkedAtSeq 1`) and for the agent-edit window; mock inheritance + `MockLoad` re-verification and `AllowCustomCmds == sha` re-consent on any argv/env/file-hash change close the fork trust boundary; `--include-vars` refusing the default `Out` and `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` + full-path `Lstat` are as declared. The P1 is the only finding that touches a guardrail the spec declares; the rest are wording/ledger items.

- [P2] [confidence 60] [blocking] §5's F8 (line 277) verifies redaction almost entirely by marker *absence*, and its positive controls stop at the snapshot's top-level keys and per-line `redacted`/`Manifest.Redacted` lists. That catches over-redaction of a *line* (a wrongly marked line shows up in `Manifest.Redacted`) but not over-redaction *within a line the table legitimately redacts*: the same `["output"]` marker results whether `confirmed` is kept (line 193) or emptied along with `rejected[].desc`; the same `["detail"]` marker results whether `detail_summary.files` is populated from the porcelain block (line 186) or always `[]`; `tree.status` "sorted list of paths" (line 198) vs an empty list; argv non-var elements retained (line 183). An implementation that over-redacts any of these destroys the evidence the export exists to carry and stays green. Add literal post-redaction payload assertions for at least: `confirmed` intact on a `match-then-adjudicate` line, `detail_summary.files` equal to the seeded porcelain paths, `tree.status` equal to the seeded path list, and a redacted argv asserted as the full expected slice (e.g. `["tool","--token=$TOKEN","src/x"]`). — evidence: spec §5 lines 183, 186, 193, 198; §8 F8 line 277.
- [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] §2 step 7's cap is `count(run.Counted) ≤ Store.MaxEvents()` (line 113); `countedType` excludes `transition`, `gate`, `cmd_call`, `converge`, `fork`, `fix_baseline`, `warn` (`internal/fsm/run/store.go:66-73`). F5's only fixture is a refusal ("store with a small `MaxEvents`"), so an implementation counting every event is green. Add the boundary control: `MaxEvents` = the child's exact counted total (accepted) with total events above it. — evidence: spec line 113; §8 F5 line 274; `internal/fsm/run/store.go:66-73`.
- [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] §6's scanner "skips an unterminated final line and fails on any other undecodable line" (lines 222-223); F9 tests an unterminated tail (skipped) and a malformed *middle* line (error) but not a malformed *terminated* final line, which must error. An implementation that skips any bad last line is green. Also missing: idempotency when the matching `id` row is not the last line (parent row, then child row, then a second `Terminal` for the parent → still one parent row) — a last-line-only lookup is green against "second call with the same view → one row". — evidence: spec lines 220-223; §8 F9 line 278.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] §5 defines substitution order (vars first, then paths, line 183) and "collisions after substitution keep both entries" for `file_hashes`; F8 has no fixture where a var value is itself an in-repo absolute path (vars-first → `$TARGET`; paths-first → `src/x`), and no `file_hashes` collision (a natural map-then-sort implementation drops one entry and is green). Also "symlinked component" (F8) should name an *intermediate* component, not `Out` itself, or an implementation that `Lstat`s only `Out` passes. — evidence: spec lines 183, 209-210; §8 F8 line 277.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] §4 "rows sorted by `(node, iter, kind, input_hash)`" (line 154) is tested only as "determinism (two calls equal)"; a sort on a different key (or a stable sort over insertion order) is deterministic and green. Assert the literal order on a fixture whose rows are inserted out of order and differ only in `kind` and then only in `input_hash`. — evidence: spec line 154; §8 F7 line 276.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] F10's "P byte-identical" postcondition covers refusals in steps 0–7 only (line 279). For step-8 seam failures (`Sidecar.Write`, `run.Create`, child `Lock`, copied/`tree`/`fix_baseline` appends) the spec's ordering (step 9 appends `fork` to P after the child is complete, line 121) implies P must also be unchanged, but only the `fork`-append failure asserts anything about P. An implementation that appends `fork` to P before writing the child is green on every other seam. Extend the byte-identical assertion to every step-8 seam. — evidence: spec lines 115-121; §8 F10 line 279.
- [P3] [confidence 50] [advisory] §2 step 0 requires "P terminal" for the escalation refusal (line 64); no F5 fixture has an attempt-3 *non-terminal* parent (a crashed/`needs_input` run with `Outcome == ""`), which must remain forkable. An implementation that drops the terminal conjunct and maps `""` to non-PASS is green. — evidence: spec lines 63-65; §8 F5 line 274.

NOTES: Reachability of 100% with the declared seams looks sound: `memStore.Lock` is exclusive (`internal/fsm/run/store_mem.go:120-134`, "already held"), so step 8's child-lock release and step 10's parent-lock release are observable through `load` in F1/F10 without a new seam; `countingStore.events` gives F6's read-once check; `gitFakes.byDir` plus a mutated `HeadSHA` covers F1/F4's `ch` discrimination and per-dir `TreeHash`/`Status`. Attempt-2 folds verified present in §8: `ch` head-advancing fixture (F1), `--from adjudicate --at-iter 0` non-restart (F1), overflow-handler freeze (F3), byte-for-byte golden rows (F9), sweep postconditions with `ERR_FORK_INCOMPLETE` (F10), idempotency by id vs target (F9), positive compatibility controls (F5), F8 fixture constraints, per-run `failLockRun` seam, `OpenFile` flag/perm assertions (F8). The two ambiguities I did not flag as testing findings but that F8's "manifest fields literal" will force a decision on: whether `Manifest.Bytes` and the `MaxBytes` check include `manifest.json` itself (self-referential size), and whether sidecars other than `workflow.yaml` may contain markers (they are copied verbatim, so the F8 constraint list should say "no marker in any sidecar" alongside `record.data`). Out of scope for this lens: concurrency of the "P's lock held for steps 0–9" claim (Architecture).

### Data-migration — attempt 3 (r4)

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

FINDINGS:
- [P1] [confidence 85] [blocking] The §6 recorder's "skip an unterminated final line" reader combined with an unguarded `AppendJSONL` append converts a repairable torn tail into a permanently undecodable line, bricking every review gate. Sequence the spec itself designs: `Terminal` → `state.AppendJSONL` partial write (crash/ENOSPC; `internal/state/state.go:21-31` is O_APPEND + one `Write(bytes+'\n')`, no fsync, no newline guard) → torn tail; "the resume path retries" → `Exists` uses the tolerant scanner, skips the fragment, returns false → `AppendJSONL` writes the new row directly after the fragment → one terminated line `<fragment>{"schemaVersion":1,...}`. That line is no longer an "unterminated final line", so `record`'s own reader now returns `ERR_RUNS_JSONL` on every read (every later `Terminal`/`Exists` fails; the retry loops forever and no FSM row can ever be written), and `runchain.ReadRuns` (`internal/runchain/runchain.go:112-114`, which tolerates nothing) fails for `taskdone` (`internal/taskdone/review.go:138`), `prready` (`internal/prready/review.go:147`), `epicready` (`internal/epicready/review.go:122`) and `reviewlog.Summarize` (`internal/reviewlog/reviewlog.go:53`) until a human hand-edits `.metareview/runs.jsonl`. Today's writers are accidentally fail-closed here (`runchain.Resolve` runs before their append and errors on the torn tail), so this is a new corruption path introduced by pairing a tolerant read with a blind append. Fix: `record`'s writer must handle the tail under its own read before appending — preserve the fragment (e.g. `runs.jsonl.torn-<ts>`) and truncate to the last `\n`, mirroring `RepairTail` (`internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go:215-243`) — or refuse with `ERR_RUNS_JSONL{reason: torn}` and never append; add an F9 row "torn tail → `Terminal` → exactly one new decodable row, fragment preserved, `runchain.ReadRuns` succeeds". — evidence: spec §6 lines 219-225 ("skips an unterminated final line (a torn `AppendJSONL`)… the resume path retries", "Idempotent by `id`"), §1 line 31 (`record` imports `internal/state`), F9 line 278 (tests the skip, never append-after-torn); `state.go:17-32`; `runchain.go:105-114`.
- [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] `InitData.WorkflowSource` is an additive field under an unchanged `SchemaVersion 1`, but payloads decode with `DisallowUnknownFields` (`internal/fsm/run/fold.go:465-467`; run spec line 57), so any binary built before the amendment folds a post-amendment log as `bad_payload` at seq 1 (run unreadable, `List` shows `Error`) rather than the `version` report run spec §5.4 promises for "an older binary". No released tag contains `internal/fsm` (latest tag v0.8.2; `main` has no fsm commits), so no shipped binary is affected — but the spec should say so in §1/§9 as the accepted exception and state the rule going forward (additive v1 fields are permitted only until the first tagged release containing `run`; after that, bump). — evidence: spec §1 lines 41-42, §9 line 302; run spec §5.4 (`docs/specs/2026-08-26-metareview-0.9.0-fsm-run-persistence.md:282-285`); `fold.go:466`.
- [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] §5's "exports are one-way… seq 1 is always redacted" rests on chain verification, not on the marker: the event envelope decoder is lenient (`internal/fsm/run/store.go:82-83`, no `DisallowUnknownFields`), so a top-level `"redacted": [...]` key is silently ignored; what actually refuses a copied-back bundle is the `prev` mismatch at seq 2 (`store.go:89`) after seq 1's bytes change. An init-only export (a run that never advanced) copied back to `audit.jsonl` passes the chain check and folds as a genuine run with `sha256:` strings as var values and `repo_root: "."` (only `file_hashes`-as-list makes `init` undecodable, and only when `allowed_cmds` is non-empty). State the mechanism and add an F8 row asserting `Store.Events` on a copied-back bundle returns `ERR_AUDIT_CHAIN` (and name the init-only edge). — evidence: spec §5 lines 166-170, 215; `store.go:76-95`; `fold.go:465-467`.
- [P3] [confidence 55] [follow-up] Export-bundle stability: `snapshot.json` redaction is a hand-maintained per-field mirror of `run.Snapshot` (§5 lines 202-205); a future `Snapshot` field is exported unredacted by default. Require a reflection test that every `run.Snapshot` field name is present in the redaction map (fail closed), analogous to the "every event type in `run.EventTypes`" rule the table already adopts. — evidence: spec §5 line 179, lines 202-205; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:190-233`.

NOTES: Row compatibility checks out: `runchain.Record` decodes leniently (`runchain.go:111-114`), the new keys are additive, `previousRunId` omitempty matches the `taskdone` writer (`internal/taskdone/review.go:47`), `scope: fsm-*` can never satisfy `escalatedForTarget`/`escalatedResetRunIDs` (`runchain.go:157, 176`), and `ChainTo` is only reached through `reviewlog.mergeRunMetadata` for review-log run ids, where a failure is a warning (`reviewlog.go:261-264`). Incomplete-fork classification cannot misclassify existing persisted data: `machine` has no `Fork` today (no `ParentRunID` writer outside `run`), so no children exist; the predicate is sound for restart (`seq = 1`, `ForkedAtSeq: 1` survives `omitempty`), non-agent-edit (`Seq == ForkedAtSeq+1` opens) and agent-edit (`fix_baseline` required, matching `fold.go:208-218`) checkpoints, and spec 2's `TreeHash == ""` branch (`internal/fsm/machine/machine.go:567`) simply becomes unreachable for forks. Copied `transition` events carrying `Origin` correctly do not set `FixEntryHead` (`fold.go:202`), which is why the fork's own `fix_baseline` is needed. `Log.Version` is constant 1 (`store.go:77`), so `Origin.Version` is stable. `TornFiles` prefix matches `List` (`store_jsonl.go:296`). Minor consistency point, not a data issue: step 7 decides "node at `From` is agent-edit" from the workflow while the incomplete predicate and fold use the stamped `StateKind`/`InitialKind`; they agree whenever stamps come from the same workflow, and a disagreement is caught by the in-memory `FoldFull` as `ERR_COPY_INVALID` before any I/O.

## Blocking Findings

- (Feasibility) [P1] [confidence 85] [blocking] The `forked` row (§2 step 9 / §6) plus "idempotent by id — a row whose `id` already exists is not written again" means a parent that is still running at fork time never gets its real terminal row: when P later reaches `fixed`/`overflow`/`failed`, `RecordTerminal` sees the existing `forked` row (verdict NEEDS_REVISION, outcome "") and skips. P's verdict map is therefore wrong for every non-terminal fork, and `runchain.Resolve`'s escalation rule (`escalatedForTarget` is first-match over all rows, `runchain.go:155-165`) never sees P's `overflow`. Either the recorder must upgrade `forked → complete` (append a second row; `ChainTo` is last-wins via `byID`, `runchain.go:126-131`, so readers tolerate it) or the forked row must be omitted and `attemptNumber` derived another way — evidence: spec §2 step 9, §6 lines 107-116; `machine.go` `terminal()` is invoked on every terminal advance (`fail`, `finish`, terminal-branch of `advance`).
- (Feasibility) [P1] [confidence 90] [blocking] `Export` redaction is defeated by its own `snapshot.json`: §5 says the exported snapshot is the **pre-redaction fold**, but `run.Snapshot` carries `Vars` (values), `NodeOutputs` (full `rejected[].desc`, full review-lenses findings), `TreeStatus`, `LastError.Detail`, `AllowedCmds[].Argv`, absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir` (`run/types.go:190-233`) — every field §5 redacts in `audit.jsonl`. Additionally the r2 preamble ("keeps every `node_output` (the snapshot must re-fold; `output_hash` is checked)") contradicts §5: after redacting `node_output.output`, `Apply` rejects the next `delta_applied` (`fold.go:120-121` `output_hash`), `gate.error.detail_summary` is an unknown field under `DisallowUnknownFields` (`fold.go:466`, `GateError` has no such field `types.go:138-143`), and `prev` chain breaks — the export cannot re-fold at all. Spec must pick one contract: redact the snapshot too (and state that the export is evidence-only, never re-foldable), or keep outputs verbatim.
- (Completeness) [P1] [85] [blocking] `Export` §5 contradicts itself on redaction: every redaction rule is stated per *event* (`init.vars` → `sha256:` unless `IncludeVars`, `gate.error.detail` emptied, `tree.status` reduced, `repo_root`/`work_dir` relativised, `allowed_cmds[].argv` var values hashed), yet the same section writes `snapshot.json` as "the **pre-redaction** fold". `run.Snapshot` carries `Vars` (verbatim values), `TreeStatus` (full porcelain), `LastError.Detail` (up to `MaxDetail` = 64 KB of uncommitted diff), absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, and `AllowedCmds` argv — so an `IncludeVars=false` bundle still ships every var value and the last gate diff. An implementer cannot satisfy both sentences; SEC-13/SEC-7 (owned by this spec) are not actually closed. Define snapshot.json as a redacted projection (same field rules) or drop it and let `fsm export` consumers re-fold `audit.jsonl` (the spec already says the snapshot "must re-fold") — evidence: spec §5 lines 95-104; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:117-233` (`Vars`, `TreeStatus`, `LastError`, `RepoRoot`, `WorkDir`, `AllowedCmds`); run spec §12 row 3 ("redact `Vars` … `tree.status`").
- (Completeness) [P1] [80] [blocking] The `ESCALATED` brake has no producer. §6 says "`runchain.Resolve` applies the same-target escalation rule to forks of an escalated run" and F9 tests "`--previous-run` resolution via `runchain.Resolve`", but no step of `Fork` (§2 steps 1-10) or `RecordTerminal` calls `Resolve`, §7 lists no error for the refusal (`Resolve` returns plain `fmt.Errorf`, not an `ERR_*`), spec 5 §2 has no `--previous-run` on any `fsm` subcommand, and nothing states which run id is passed as `PreviousRunID` (the parent) or what happens when `attemptNumber` would exceed `maxAttempts` (existing writers force `ESCALATED` via `verdictForCounts`; this spec's verdict map never does). Owned items "`fsmRunRecord` + verdict map + `ESCALATED`" (ledger row 3, SCP3-1, CMP3-10) therefore have a mapping but no enforcement contract — evidence: spec §6 lines 115-117, §8 F9, §7; `internal/runchain/runchain.go:64-91` (errors on `previous run … already escalated` / `same target already escalated`); `internal/taskdone/review.go:161,450`; spec 5 §2 lines 24-40.
- (Scope and alignment) [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] §6 writes a `forked` runs.jsonl row at fork time, contradicting locked C27 / plan §1.9 ("appended once, at terminal, by `advance`"), and the deviation is not ledgered anywhere (this spec has no ledger; the r2 header only says "a `forked` row so children can chain"). Combined with §6's own rule "Idempotent: a row whose `id` already exists is not written again", the parent's real terminal outcome is then never recorded: step 1 allows forking any transition checkpoint (a non-terminal, in-flight P included — "A terminal P is allowed" is stated as the special case), step 9 writes P a `forked`/`NEEDS_REVISION` row, and when P later reaches `fixed`/`clean` the machine's terminal call (spec 2 r4: "`Deps.Terminal` is idempotent and runs on every terminal advance") is a no-op. `metareview status` and `runchain` see a permanent NEEDS_REVISION for a passing run. Either the forked row must be updatable (breaking "idempotent by id") or it must not exist (honouring C27) — the spec currently claims both. — evidence: target §2 step 1 (l.36-40), step 9 (l.65-66), §6 (l.107-117); plan §0 C27, §1.9 (l.321-326); spec 2 r4 header "Deps.Terminal is idempotent and runs on every terminal advance"; spec 2 §7 M9.
- (Scope and alignment) [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] §6's verdict map sends `overflow` → **ESCALATED**, but plan §1.6 (the mapping C27/§1.9 binds to: `"verdict": <§1.6 mapping>`) locks `overflow` → NEEDS_REVISION, and the change is unledgered. It is not a cosmetic relabel: `target.id = <workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>` is inherited verbatim by every fork (step 7 copies `InitData` incl. `BaseSHA`), so an ESCALATED parent row makes `runchain.escalatedForTarget` match every fork and every re-`init` on the same base; under CLAUDE.md's completion rule ("ESCALATED stops same-target retries") the design's prescribed recovery from a safety stop (design §10.1 resume, §16 overflow handler = "fallback action") becomes a forbidden same-target retry. The accompanying sentence "`runchain.Resolve` applies the same-target escalation rule to forks of an escalated run" is not traceable to any step: nothing in §2 calls `Resolve`, spec 5's fork command has no `--previous-run`, and the only `Resolve` mention is the F9 test row — so the claimed enforcement does not exist while the ESCALATED label does. — evidence: target §6 (l.115-117), §8 F9; plan §1.6 table (l.275-286), §1.9 (l.323); `internal/runchain/runchain.go:49-92,155-165`; CLAUDE.md "Completion Rule"; spec 5 §2 fork command line.
- (Architecture) [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] The `forked` runs.jsonl row poisons the parent's real terminal record. §6 makes `RecordTerminal` "idempotent: a row whose `id` already exists is not written again", and §2 step 9 writes a `forked` row (`status: forked`, `verdict: NEEDS_REVISION`, `outcome: ""`) whenever P has no row. Forking a non-terminal P (judge swap on a run parked at NEEDS_INPUT, or a ctx-cancelled run — §2 step 1 only says a terminal P is "allowed", so live parents are the default) therefore guarantees that when P later reaches `fixed`/`clean`, the machine's `Deps.Terminal` call (machine.go:833, :789, :544) is a no-op and P is durably recorded as NEEDS_REVISION forever; `runchain.ChainTo` (runchain.go:127-131, last-row-wins map) and `escalatedForTarget` (:155) then reason over a wrong verdict, and `metareview status` shows the wrong outcome. Either refuse non-terminal parents (also removes the "P advances while I copy it" race) or key idempotency on `(id, status)` with a `forked` row superseded by a `complete` row (append-only + last-wins is already how `runchain` reads). — evidence: spec §2 step 9 (lines 65-66), §6 (lines 107-108, 116); machine.go:883-888; runchain.go:127-153
- (Architecture) [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] `AcceptWorkflowChange` has no structural-compatibility invariant, and the fold cannot supply one because `run` treats states as opaque. §2 step 1 checks `From` against **P's** workflow only; step 2 then parses arbitrary new bytes and step 7 copies P's events verbatim with their old `State` stamps. If the new workflow renames/removes `From` (or any state in the copied prefix), the child folds cleanly, `Open` succeeds, and the first `Advance` hits `w.NodeFor(state) == nil` → `Outgoing(state)` empty → `TerminalFor` nil → `s.fail(failures[0], head)` indexes an empty slice (machine.go:768-769) — a panic on a fork the spec accepted. Related holes: step 7's "re-validate `node_output` with the child workflow's kind `Decode`" is undefined when the node is gone; `Parse` may yield a different `w.Name` so `RunID(w.Name,…)`/`scope: fsm-<workflow>` diverge from P and `runchain.Resolve` rejects the chain ("ancestor run … does not match", runchain.go:70-73); and `Resolve(effective)` refuses P's stored vars that the new workflow no longer declares (resolve.go:19-23) so a var removal makes the fork impossible rather than dropping the var. Require: `w.Name == P.Workflow`, every state stamped in `[2..seq]` (at least `From`) exists in the child workflow with the same node `Kind`, and P's vars filtered to the new declared set (or `ERR_WORKFLOW_INCOMPATIBLE{reason}`). — evidence: spec §2 steps 1-3, 7 (lines 37-47, 58-60); machine.go:687-771; workflow.go:701; resolve.go:18-23
- (Intent preservation) [P1] [confidence 100] [blocking] Export's "leaks no secrets" intent is inverted by `snapshot.json`: §5 (L100-101) writes the **pre-redaction** fold, but `run.Snapshot` carries every field the audit redaction removes — `Vars` (raw values, so `IncludeVars=false` is a no-op), `LastError.Detail` (the `commit_exists` failure embeds the full working diff up to 64 KB), `TreeStatus` (full porcelain), absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, and `NodeOutputs` with `Rejected[].desc` intact. Plan §3.5/SEC-13 says the export *redacts*; r2 ships an unredacted twin beside the redacted log in `docs/metareview/fsm/<id>/` (a committed path). The `output_hash` re-fold argument justifies redacting the snapshot too or omitting it, not shipping it raw. — evidence: target §5 L96-104; `internal/fsm/run/types.go` `Snapshot{Vars, RepoRoot, WorkDir, TreeStatus, NodeOutputs, LastError}`; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:173-180` (gate failure → `LastError` with Detail); `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go:114-122` (detail = status + working diff); plan §3.5, §0.2 SEC-13.
- (Intent preservation) [P1] [confidence 75] [blocking] The GATE_FAILED resume recipe is inverted for the ordering the design and plan prescribe ("fix the commit, resume"). Step 6 adopts the FEA-N4 ancestor rule for `agent-edit` precisely so "commits on top are the node's own work", but step 8 then appends `tree{Head: HEAD}` + `fix_baseline{Head: HEAD}`, which the fold makes the new `FixEntryHead`; `commit_exists` counts `FixEntryHead..HEAD`, so an operator who commits *before* forking (the plan §1.7 step 6 order: "commit, `advance`, `record`, `advance`") gets `ERR_NO_COMMIT` again with no way out short of an extra commit. F2 only tests the fork-then-commit order, so the spec is self-consistent but silently drops the commit-first path the ancestor rule exists to serve. Either baseline at the checkpoint head `ch` (what the rule's rationale implies) or state that commits must come *after* the fork and drop the ancestor allowance. — evidence: target §2 step 6 L52-54, step 8 L63-64, F2 L129; plan §1.7 step 6 and §0.2 FEA-N4; `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:99` (tree updates Head), `:216-218` (fix_baseline sets FixEntryHead); `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go:106-114`.
- (Security) [P1] [confidence 90] [blocking] `snapshot.json` nullifies every export redaction. §5 says the exported `snapshot.json` is "the **pre-redaction** fold written by the exporter" and F8 asserts "snapshot equals the pre-redaction fold". `run.Snapshot` carries, in plaintext, exactly the fields §5 redacts in the events: `Vars` (the var values `init.vars` is hashed to hide), `AllowedCmds[].Argv`, absolute `RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`, full `TreeStatus` (porcelain), `LastError.Detail` (for `commit_exists` this is the porcelain status plus the working diff), and every `NodeOutputs` value (rejected descs, full lens findings). Anyone who reads the committed `docs/metareview/fsm/<run-id>/snapshot.json` gets everything `audit.jsonl` was scrubbed of. Fix: define a snapshot redaction that mirrors the event redaction (Vars→`sha256:` unless `IncludeVars`, argv-from-vars, paths relative, `TreeStatus`→file list, `LastError.Detail`→summary, per-kind `NodeOutputs` trimming) and drop the "pre-redaction" wording; the manifest already declares `chain: "redacted"` so the hash mismatch is accepted. — evidence: spec §5 (lines 96-97, F8 row); `internal/fsm/run/types.go:179` (`Vars`), `:184` (`AllowedCmds`), `:186-187` (`RepoRoot`/`WorkDir`), `:197` (`TreeStatus`), `:208` (`NodeOutputs`), `:212` (`LastError`); `internal/fsm/run/fold.go:99` (status folded), `:179` (LastError folded); `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go` `commitExists` detail = `--- status ---\n%s--- working diff ---\n%s`.
- (Security) [P1] [confidence 85] [blocking] Argv-from-vars redaction is incomplete and trivially bypassed. §5 replaces only `init.allowed_cmds[].argv` elements *equal to* a var value. - (attempt 3, Security) [P1] [confidence 80] [blocking] The declared torn-tail tolerance of `runs.jsonl` (§6, spec lines 221-223: "skips an unterminated final line (a torn `AppendJSONL`) … a `Terminal` error leaves the transition durable and the resume path retries") is defeated by the spec's own write path. `record` imports `internal/state` (§1 line 31) and the writer it follows (`internal/taskdone/review.go:193`) is `state.AppendJSONL`, which opens with `O_APPEND` and writes `json+"\n"` without checking that the file already ends in `\n` (`/Users/dsifry/Developer/metareview/internal/state/state.go:21-31`). Sequence the spec itself designs: `Terminal` → `state.AppendJSONL` partial write (crash/ENOSPC; `internal/state/state.go:21-31` is O_APPEND + one `Write(bytes+'\n')`, no fsync, no newline guard) → torn tail; "the resume path retries" → `Exists` uses the tolerant scanner, skips the fragment, returns false → `AppendJSONL` writes the new row directly after the fragment → one terminated line `<fragment>{"schemaVersion":1,...}`. That line is no longer an "unterminated final line", so `record`'s own reader now returns `ERR_RUNS_JSONL` on every read (every later `Terminal`/`Exists` fails; the retry loops forever and no FSM row can ever be written), and `runchain.ReadRuns` (`internal/runchain/runchain.go:112-114`, which tolerates nothing) fails for `taskdone` (`internal/taskdone/review.go:138`), `prready` (`internal/prready/review.go:147`), `epicready` (`internal/epicready/review.go:122`) and `reviewlog.Summarize` (`internal/reviewlog/reviewlog.go:53`) until a human hand-edits `.metareview/runs.jsonl`. Today's writers are accidentally fail-closed here (`runchain.Resolve` runs before their append and errors on the torn tail), so this is a new corruption path introduced by pairing a tolerant read with a blind append. Fix: `record`'s writer must handle the tail under its own read before appending — preserve the fragment (e.g. `runs.jsonl.torn-<ts>`) and truncate to the last `\n`, mirroring `RepairTail` (`internal/fsm/run/store_jsonl.go:215-243`) — or refuse with `ERR_RUNS_JSONL{reason: torn}` and never append; add an F9 row "torn tail → `Terminal` → exactly one new decodable row, fragment preserved, `runchain.ReadRuns` succeeds". — evidence: spec §6 lines 219-225 ("skips an unterminated final line (a torn `AppendJSONL`)… the resume path retries", "Idempotent by `id`"), §1 line 31 (`record` imports `internal/state`), F9 line 278 (tests the skip, never append-after-torn); `state.go:17-32`; `runchain.go:105-114`.

## Advisory Findings

- (Feasibility) [P2] [confidence 85] [advisory] `Export` cannot copy `audit.torn-*.bin` through the declared seams: `Sidecar.List/Read` refuse any name starting with `audit.` (`sidecar.go:22-23`, `checkSidecarArgs` → `ERR_SIDECAR{name}`), and `RunStore` exposes no torn-file reader. The only route is direct FS under `Store.Root()`, which `MemSidecar`/`memStore` cannot exercise — F8 "torn files copied" is not reachable at 100% with the harness fakes without a new seam (e.g. `Sidecar.ReadRaw` or a `Store.TornFiles(runID)`).
- (Feasibility) [P2] [confidence 80] [advisory] Step 7 only re-validates `node_output` payloads, but with `AcceptWorkflowChange` other copied events fail inside `Append`: a copied `cmd_call`/`overflow_handler` whose command was removed/renamed hits `unsanctioned_cmd` (`fold.go:164-171`, child `AllowedCmds` are the *new* list) and surfaces as `ERR_APPEND_REJECTED`, not `ERR_COPY_INVALID`; a copied `node_output` whose node name no longer exists has no kind to `Decode` with. Define `ERR_COPY_INVALID{seq, reason}` for these (and note the child dir is left behind in both cases).
- (Feasibility) [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] Step 6's ancestor rule for `agent-edit` is hollow: `fix_baseline.Head` must equal `Snapshot.Head`, which the immediately preceding `tree{Head: HEAD}` sets to HEAD (`fold.go:212-216`, `:98-100`), and copied transitions with `Origin` never set `FixEntryHead` (`fold.go:202`). So `FixEntryHead == HEAD` always, and `commit_exists` counts `FixEntryHead..HEAD` (`gates.go:97-108`) — any commit made *before* the fork is invisible; "commit, then fork --from fix" yields `ERR_NO_COMMIT` again. F2 only tests commit-after-fork. Either document this ordering rule in spec 5's recovery recipe or drop the ancestor allowance (exact-head everywhere).
- (Feasibility) [P2] [confidence 70] [advisory] Checkpoint rule is ambiguous for looping workflows: `sdlc-loop` loops back to its initial state `discover` (`workflows/sdlc-loop.yaml:16`), so `--from discover` with `AtIter` nil is both "latest transition with To == discover" and "From == Initial → seq = 1". Likewise `ERR_VAR_FROZEN{name, state}` must define which state is reported when several ran and reference the var (`adjudicate` and `verify` both reference `$JUDGE`; F3 asserts `adjudicate`) — say "first in `w.States` order".
- (Feasibility) [P3] [confidence 80] [advisory] Torn parent: `load` only sets `m.torn` (`machine.go` load), so Fork would create the whole child (Create, sidecar, copies, tree) and fail only at step 9 with `ERR_AUDIT_TORN` from `prepareAppend` (`store.go:119-121`), orphaning a child with no `fork` link. Refuse up front like `Advance`/`Record` do. Also "version-selected file" in §3 has no store support (single `audit.jsonl`, `Log.Version` fixed at 1, `store.go:77`); `VerifyOrigin` should define a reason for a parent whose chain fails to parse (`ERR_AUDIT_CHAIN`) — currently only `parent_missing|hash_mismatch`.
- (Feasibility) [P3] [confidence 70] [advisory] `runchain.Resolve` requires every ancestor to share scope **and** `sameTarget` (DeepEqual, `runchain.go:70-73`); the fsm target id is `<workflow>@<base_sha[:12]>`, so a fork accepted with a different workflow name (`AcceptWorkflowChange`) or a child whose `init` records a different `base_sha` cannot resolve `--previous-run`, and `attemptNumber = 1 + parent's` silently degrades to 1 when the parent row is absent (`ReadRuns` default, `runchain.go:115-117`). Specify the target for forks (root's target?) and whether a mismatch is an error.

- (Architecture) [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] Incomplete forks are openable and fail later with an unrelated error; the parent `fork` event is a second, weaker source of truth. - (Intent preservation) [P2] [confidence 75] [advisory] `Diff`'s cell-by-cell verdict comparison degrades for the headline use case (§17 adjudicator validation). No released tag contains `internal/fsm` (latest tag v0.8.2; `main` has no fsm commits), so no shipped binary is affected — but the spec should say so in §1/§9 as the accepted exception and state the rule going forward (additive v1 fields are permitted only until the first tagged release containing `run`; after that, bump). — evidence: spec §1 lines 41-42, §9 line 302; run spec §5.4 (`docs/specs/2026-08-26-metareview-0.9.0-fsm-run-persistence.md:282-285`); `fold.go:466`.
- (attempt 3, Data-migration) [P3] [confidence 60] [advisory] §5's "exports are one-way… seq 1 is always redacted" rests on chain verification, not on the marker: the event envelope decoder is lenient (`internal/fsm/run/store.go:82-83`, no `DisallowUnknownFields`), so a top-level `"redacted": [...]` key is silently ignored; what actually refuses a copied-back bundle is the `prev` mismatch at seq 2 (`store.go:89`) after seq 1's bytes change. An init-only export (a run that never advanced) copied back to `audit.jsonl` passes the chain check and folds as a genuine run with `sha256:` strings as var values and `repo_root: "."` (only `file_hashes`-as-list makes `init` undecodable, and only when `allowed_cmds` is non-empty). State the mechanism and add an F8 row asserting `Store.Events` on a copied-back bundle returns `ERR_AUDIT_CHAIN` (and name the init-only edge). — evidence: spec §5 lines 166-170, 215; `store.go:76-95`; `fold.go:465-467`.

## Follow-up Findings

- (Feasibility) [P3] [confidence 65] [follow-up] `RecordTerminal(repoRoot)` has no clock: `createdAt` comes from `View.Snapshot.CreatedAt` but `updatedAt` needs "now", forcing `time.Now` inside `machine` (every other timestamp is `Deps.Clock`). Take a `Clock` (or use `deps.Clock` by making it a method on `Deps`) so F9 can assert literal rows.
- (Completeness) [P3] [70] [follow-up] Under-declared fields: `Diff.Report.SameWorkflow` is undefined (different names already error, so it must mean `WorkflowHash` equality — say so); `Manifest.Redacted []string` has no vocabulary, `SourceHead` no source (snapshot `Head` vs exporter's git HEAD), `SchemaVersion` no value; `ExportOptions.MaxBytes` measured on what (sum of written files, pre- or post-redaction); `ForkResult.Rebaselined` is always true after r2 ("every fork rebaselines"); `VerifyOrigin.Reason` has no value for an unreadable (torn/invalid) parent log; `ERR_VAR_FROZEN{state}` when several states froze the var — which one — evidence: spec §3, §4 line 79, §5 lines 91-93, §2 step 3, §2 line 34.
- (Completeness) [P3] [70] [follow-up] Signature drift vs implemented code: `Parse(raw, Kinds.Info())` — `workflow.Parse` takes `Options{Kinds}`; `Resolve(effective, false)` for a calibration parent bypasses the pinned-var refusal that `Resolve(…, true)` already implements (`CodeCalibrationPinned`) so step 3 re-implements it by hand — pass `P.Calibration`; `RecordTerminal(repoRoot)` has no clock for `updatedAt` (100% tests need a fake); the `ForkedAtSeq` precondition owned by row 3 ("== copied events + 1") is only implicit in step 7 — evidence: spec §1, §2 step 3, §6; `internal/fsm/workflow/workflow.go:88-90,161`; `internal/fsm/workflow/resolve.go:18-30`; run spec §7 line 303, §12 row 3.
- (Intent preservation) [P3] [confidence 50] [follow-up] `record.data` is kept verbatim on export while everything else agent-supplied is capped or reduced; `AllowedCmd.FileHashes` keys and pinned `argv[0]` remain absolute paths although `repo_root`/`work_dir` are relativised. Minor leak surface relative to the stated "no secrets" goal. — evidence: target §5 L101-103; `internal/fsm/run/types.go` `AllowedCmd{Argv, FileHashes}`.
- (Data-migration) [P2] [confidence 70] [follow-up] `Origin.Version` / "version-selected file": `RunStore` has no versioned read (`EventsWithLines(runID)` only; `parseLines` hard-codes `Version: 1`). Under run §5.4's `Migrate` contract `EventsWithLines` targets the highest-version file, so after a parent migration every `Origin{Version: 1}` hash is checked against v2 lines and the whole prefix reports `hash_mismatch`. Either add `EventsWithLines(runID, version)` to the store contract now or specify the fallback reason (`version_unavailable`, not `hash_mismatch`). — evidence: spec §3 lines 72-74; `internal/fsm/run/store.go:51-60,77`; run spec §5.4.

The §9 handoff row exists, but spec 5's text is stale — evidence: `docs/specs/2026-08-27-metareview-0.9.0-fsm-cli.md:112`; fork spec §1, §9 l.245.

- (attempt 2, Intent preservation) [P3] [confidence 50] [follow-up] `mock` row = `Snapshot.Mock != ""` ignores `MockTainted`; a tainted hand-built log would record `mock: false`. Use `Mock != "" || MockTainted`. — evidence: target §6 L188; run spec §4.8 (`MockTainted`).
- (attempt 2, Security) [P3] [confidence 70] [follow-up] Incomplete-fork detection (`Seq ≤ ForkedAtSeq`) misses two crash windows: after `tree` but before `fix_baseline` (agent-edit `From`) the child opens "complete" and `commit_exists` fails closed as `ERR_GATE_INAPPLICABLE` (safe but misleading); between `run.Create` and `Sidecar.Write` the child surfaces `ERR_SIDECAR{missing}` rather than `ERR_FORK_INCOMPLETE`. Define incomplete as "missing the rebaseline `tree`, or missing `fix_baseline` when `From`'s node is agent-edit" and state the error precedence. — evidence: spec §2 steps 7–8, 10; `internal/fsm/gate/gates.go:99-101`.
- (attempt 2, Data-migration) [P3] [confidence 75] [follow-up] Export shape: the `redacted` marker is a top-level Event key that `parseLines` silently ignores (`store.go:82-85`, no `DisallowUnknownFields`), so "one-way" holds only because the redacted `init` breaks seq 2's `prev` check, not because of the marker; redacted `data` gains fields that no `run` payload type has (`gate.error.detail_summary`, `llm_call.error_truncated`) and `tree.status` changes from a string to a "sorted file list" (type unspecified); `snapshot.json` carries `schemaVersion: 1` with no in-file redaction marker (only the manifest says so). Document the export payload schema (or state that `data` is schema-free evidence) and add a `redacted` marker inside `snapshot.json` — evidence: spec §5 lines 136–140, 143–161, 163; `event.go:122-126,95-106,76-81`; `types.go:190-191`.

- (attempt 3, Scope and alignment) [P3] [confidence 50] [follow-up] Reciprocity is fine from spec 3's side, but spec 2 §8's mirror row "Spec 3 r4 (owned there, received here)" also bundles `ERR_CMDS_NOT_ALLOWED` `Fields["cmds_json"]`, `Machine.Judge`, and `OpenOptions{ReadOnly}` — items spec 5 §9 attributes to spec 2 itself and which fork spec §1 (lines 40-53) correctly does not claim. The over-attribution lives in spec 2's ledger, not this artifact; flag for spec 2's next revision. — evidence: fsm-core.md §8 last bullet; fsm-cli.md §9 "spec 2 §8 (received)"; fork §1.
- (attempt 3, Intent preservation) [P3] [confidence 50] [follow-up] Diff usefulness vs design §16's headline swap ("the most compelling swap — your own adjudicator — is a shell command"): `Diff` compares `llm_call` rows only (§4, lines 140-156); a `cmd`-kind adjudicator emits `cmd_call` + `node_output`, so its verdicts never appear in `Calls` — only `Outcomes`/`Transitions` differ. Moreover §2 step 2's compatibility invariant requires `From` itself to keep the same kind and exec (line 79-80), so swapping a built-in adjudicate for a `cmd` adjudicator is not a fork at all but a fresh `init`, with `CommonPrefixSeq` = 1. Plan §3.5 also had only `llm_calls`, so this is consistent with the accepted plan, not a loss — but a `NodeOutputs`-level row (confirmed-set symmetric difference by `BugID`) would close the §16 story and belongs with the `Subject` follow-up to spec 4. — evidence: fork.md §2 (79-82), §4 (140-156); design §16 "Why include it"; plan §3.5 (400-405)
- (attempt 3, Security) [P3] [confidence 55] [follow-up] `ERR_RUN_ESCALATED` requires "P terminal" (§2 step 0, L64). A non-terminal attempt-3 P (abandoned or stuck at `needs_input`) is therefore forkable without bound into attempt-4+ children; each non-PASS leaf records `escalated` and is itself refused, which is consistent with the ledgered lineage-depth rule, but §6's "human reset" sentence (L245-246) should name this case so the limit is not over-read. — evidence: spec L63-65, L241-246
- (attempt 3, Data-migration) [P3] [confidence 55] [follow-up] Export-bundle stability: `snapshot.json` redaction is a hand-maintained per-field mirror of `run.Snapshot` (§5 lines 202-205); a future `Snapshot` field is exported unredacted by default. Require a reflection test that every `run.Snapshot` field name is present in the redaction map (fail closed), analogous to the "every event type in `run.EventTypes`" rule the table already adopts. — evidence: spec §5 line 179, lines 202-205; `internal/fsm/run/types.go:190-233`.
